1	# CipherBox
2	
3	## What This Is
4	
5	CipherBox is a production-grade, privacy-first encrypted cloud storage platform using IPFS/IPNS and Web3Auth. It provides zero-knowledge file storage with user-to-user sharing, link sharing, client-side search, multi-factor authentication, file versioning, conflict detection, recycle bin, and cross-platform desktop apps (macOS, Windows, Linux). The server is cryptographically unable to access user data.
6	
7	## Core Value
8	
9	**Zero-knowledge privacy**: Files are encrypted client-side before leaving the device, and encryption keys exist only in client memory. The server is cryptographically unable to access user data.
10	
11	## Current Milestone: v1.1 IPFS Infrastructure
12	
13	**Goal:** Make CipherBox more IPFS-native — replace delegated-ipfs.dev, migrate selected vault state to IPFS/IPNS, add BYO-IPFS server-relay support, and establish performance baselines.
14	
15	**Target features:**
16	
17	- Reliable IPNS resolution (replace delegated-ipfs.dev with self-hosted or alternative provider)
18	- Reduce database dependence where feasible — migrate vault crypto material to IPFS while retaining `folder_ipns`, shares, device approvals, quota tracking, and the DB fallback for `encryptedRootFolderKey` in v1.1
19	- Bring-your-own IPFS node support via server-relay flow (client-direct deferred to v1.2)
20	- Comprehensive performance baselines (API, client, IPFS/IPNS latency, end-to-end user journeys)
21	
22	## Requirements
23	
24	### Validated (Milestone 1 — Staging MVP)
25	
26	- Web3Auth authentication (email, OAuth, magic link, external wallet) — v0.1
27	- Client-side AES-256-GCM encryption + ECIES key wrapping — v0.1
28	- IPFS file storage via Kubo with IPNS metadata — v0.1
29	- Full file/folder CRUD with 20-level hierarchy — v0.1
30	- File browser web UI with terminal aesthetic — v0.1
31	- Multi-device sync via IPNS polling (30s) — v0.1
32	- TEE auto-republishing via Phala Cloud — v0.1
33	- macOS desktop client with Tauri + FUSE mount — v0.1
34	- Vault export with standalone recovery tool — v0.1
35	- CI/CD pipeline with staging deployment — v0.1
36	
37	### Validated (Milestone 2 — Production v1.0)
38	
39	- User-to-user file/folder sharing with ECIES key re-wrapping (read-only, instant via public key) — v1.0
40	- Link sharing with URL-fragment decryption keys (authenticated invite model) — v1.0
41	- Client-side encrypted search index (MiniSearch + IndexedDB) — v1.0
42	- MFA via Core Kit MPC (device shares, recovery phrase, cross-device approval) — v1.0
43	- File version history with restore and retention policy — v1.0
44	- Optimistic concurrency conflict detection on IPNS publishes — v1.0
45	- Recycle bin with 30-day soft-delete retention and CID unpinning — v1.0
46	- Windows desktop app with WinFsp virtual filesystem — v1.0
47	- Linux desktop app with FUSE mount (AppImage + deb) — v1.0
48	- AES-256-CTR streaming encryption for in-browser media playback — v1.0
49	- Per-file IPNS metadata split (content updates decoupled from folder publishes) — v1.0
50	- Cross-platform E2E test matrix (macOS, Windows, Linux) — v1.0
51	
52	### Active (Milestone 3 — IPFS Infrastructure v1.1)
53	
54	See `.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md` for full requirements.
55	
56	#### IPNS Reliability
57	
58	- [ ] Replace delegated-ipfs.dev with reliable IPNS resolution
59	- [ ] Sub-2s resolution latency, >99.5% availability
60	
61	#### Database Minimization
62	
63	- [x] Move rootFolderKey to IPFS vault blob v2 format (DB crypto columns dropped entirely) — Validated in Phase 20: Vault Migration
64	- [x] Deprecate encryptedRootIpnsPrivateKey (HKDF-derivable, DB column dropped) — Validated in Phase 20: Vault Migration
65	
66	#### BYO-IPFS
67	
68	- [ ] User-configurable IPFS node endpoint
69	- [ ] Server-relay upload to user's node (client-direct deferred to v1.2)
70	- [ ] Quota and conflict detection strategy for BYO mode
71	
72	#### Performance Baselines
73	
74	- [ ] API endpoint response time baselines
75	- [ ] IPFS/IPNS publish and resolve latency baselines
76	- [ ] Client-side encryption throughput baselines
77	- [ ] End-to-end user journey timing baselines
78	
79	### Out of Scope (Milestone 3 — v1.1)
80	
81	- Encrypted Productivity Suite (billing, teams, doc editors, signing) — deferred to Milestone 4 (v2.0)
82	- Mobile apps (iOS/Android) — deferred to Milestone 4+
83	- Real-time collaborative editing — deferred to Milestone 4+
84	- Offline write queue / selective sync — deferred to Milestone 4+
85	- Full-text content search — encrypted index leaks access patterns
86	- CRDT-based IPNS inbox — research only this milestone, implement in future if viable
87	- eIDAS/QES compliance — requires certified CA
88	- SSO/LDAP — enterprise scope
89	
90	## Context
91	
92	**Current State (v1.0 shipped 2026-03-05):**
93	
94	- 423,869 lines of TypeScript + Rust across 698 source files
95	- NestJS API, React 18 web app, Tauri desktop (macOS/Windows/Linux)
96	- 155 plans executed across 35 phase directories (M1 + M2)
97	- Staging deployed at api-staging.cipherbox.cc / app-staging.cipherbox.cc
98	- 8 Playwright E2E test suites + 4 desktop E2E script pairs
99	
100	**Technical Environment:**
101	
102	- IPFS via Kubo for file storage and pinning
103	- IPNS for mutable metadata pointers (per-folder + per-file)
104	- Web3Auth Core Kit MPC for deterministic ECDSA key derivation
105	- Phala Cloud for TEE auto-republishing (3-hour interval)
106	
107	**Key Architecture (evolved through M1 + M2):**
108	
109	- Client-side encryption only — server is zero-knowledge relay
110	- Per-folder + per-file IPNS keypairs (HKDF-derived)
111	- ECIES key re-wrapping for zero-knowledge sharing
112	- Optimistic concurrency on IPNS publishes (sequence number checks)
113	- Deterministic vault IPNS derivation (self-sovereign recovery)
114	- FUSE-T SMB backend on macOS (NFS had kernel bugs)
115	
116	## Constraints
117	
118	- **File size**: 100 MB max — browser memory limits
119	- **Storage quota**: 500 MiB free tier — IPFS storage management
120	- **Files per folder**: 1,000 max — UI performance
121	- **Folder depth**: 20 levels max — traversal performance
122	- **Sync latency**: ~30 seconds — IPNS polling interval
123	- **Tech stack**: NestJS backend, React 18 frontend, Tauri desktop — per specifications
124	- **Auth provider**: Web3Auth Core Kit MPC — deterministic key derivation requirement
125	- **IPFS provider**: Kubo (self-hosted), BYO-IPFS support (Kubo, PSA, Pinata)
126	
127	## Key Decisions
128	
129	| Decision                                    | Rationale                                                  | Outcome |
130	| ------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------- | ------- |
131	| Full-stack vertical build order             | Test features end-to-end as they're built                  | Good    |
132	| Web + cross-platform desktop for v1.0       | Complete user experience across all platforms              | Good    |
133	| TEE republishing required for v1.0          | Zero-downtime vault access guarantee                       | Good    |
134	| Core Kit MPC replaces PnP Modal             | Self-hosted identity provider, MFA foundation              | Good    |
135	| Per-file IPNS metadata split                | Decouple content updates from folder publishes             | Good    |
136	| AES-256-CTR for streaming media             | Byte-range decryption enables in-browser playback          | Good    |
137	| Optimistic concurrency via sequence numbers | Lightweight conflict detection without distributed locks   | Good    |
138	| FUSE-T SMB backend (not NFS)                | macOS NFS kernel bug blocked WRITE RPCs for new files      | Good    |
139	| Encrypted recycle bin on IPFS               | Client-side retention enforcement, CID unpinning on delete | Good    |
140	| SIWE wallet login with hashed identifiers   | Privacy-preserving auth, unified identity across methods   | Good    |
141	| Decimal phase numbering for insertions      | Clear insertion semantics without renumbering              | Good    |
142	
143	---
144	
145	Last updated: 2026-06-13 after Phase 44 IPNS Conflict Handling completed. UAT-audit gap-closure phases 42-44 all done: Phase 42 ownership-guarded unpin (cross-user refcounting, transactional quota decrement, pending-unpins outbox); Phase 43 FUSE write durability (fsynced ciphertext journal, replay-on-mount, mkdir-conflict retry, park notifications across macOS/Linux/Windows); Phase 44 IPNS conflict handling (three-way folder merge on 409 with publishedChildren convergence, file-record CAS with loser-becomes-version). v1.1 milestone complete.
146

1	# Requirements: CipherBox v1.1 IPFS Infrastructure
2	
3	**Defined:** 2026-03-07
4	**Core Value:** Zero-knowledge privacy -- files encrypted client-side, server never sees plaintext
5	
6	## v1.1 Requirements
7	
8	Requirements for IPFS infrastructure milestone. Each maps to roadmap phases.
9	
10	### IPNS Reliability
11	
12	- [x] **IPNS-01**: Self-hosted Someguy deployed alongside Kubo, replacing delegated-ipfs.dev as primary IPNS routing provider
13	- [x] **IPNS-02**: IPNS resolution uses DB-first strategy with async Kubo DHT verification via self-hosted Someguy
14	- [x] **IPNS-03**: Recovery tool resolves IPNS via self-hosted Someguy instead of delegated-ipfs.dev
15	- [x] **IPNS-04**: System degrades gracefully when DHT resolution is slow (timeout + DB fallback within 2s)
16	
17	### Vault Migration
18	
19	- [x] **VAULT-01**: rootFolderKey embedded in IPFS vault blob v2 format (ECIES-wrapped in blob header)
20	- [x] **VAULT-02**: Client reads rootFolderKey exclusively from IPFS v2 blob on login (DB crypto columns dropped)
21	- [x] **VAULT-03**: All vaults migrated to v2 — dead migration code removed, DB columns dropped
22	- [x] **VAULT-04**: encryptedRootIpnsPrivateKey column dropped from vaults table (HKDF-derivable)
23	- [x] **VAULT-05**: Recovery tool updated to parse vault blob v2 format
24	- [x] **VAULT-06**: Desktop app (Rust) parses vault blob v2 format
25	
26	### BYO-IPFS
27	
28	- [x] **BYO-01**: RemotePinningProvider implements standard IPFS Pinning Service API (pin/unpin/status)
29	- [x] **BYO-02**: DualPinProvider pins to both CipherBox node and user's configured node
30	- [x] **BYO-03**: Per-user IPFS config stored server-side (endpoint URL, encrypted auth token, provider type)
31	- [x] **BYO-04**: Settings UI for configuring custom IPFS node endpoint and credentials
32	- [x] **BYO-05**: Connection test endpoint validates user's IPFS node is reachable and API-compatible
33	- [x] **BYO-06**: All IPNS publishes still route through CipherBox API regardless of BYO config
34	- [x] **BYO-07**: Quota tracking becomes advisory for BYO users with clear UI indication
35	
36	### Performance Baselines
37	
38	- [x] **PERF-01**: IPFS/IPNS duration histograms added to Prometheus (publish, resolve, pin, cat)
39	- [x] **PERF-02**: API endpoint p50/p95/p99 baselines defined per critical route
40	- [x] **PERF-03**: Kubo Prometheus endpoint scraped for node health metrics (peers, bandwidth, datastore)
41	- [x] **PERF-04**: TEE republish batch duration histogram added
42	- [x] **PERF-05**: Client-side timing instrumentation for encrypt/decrypt, upload/download, IPNS operations
43	- [x] **PERF-06**: End-to-end user journey timing captured (login-to-vault, upload-to-visible, share-to-accessible)
44	- [x] **PERF-07**: Vitest-based SDK load harness simulating concurrent users (upload, download, publish, resolve)
45	- [x] **PERF-08**: Capacity thresholds documented with scaling recommendations
46	- [x] **PERF-09**: Upload operations optimized via concurrent SDK pin orchestration (Promise.allSettled) and Kubo pebbleds datastore, with before/after baselines documented
47	
48	### SDK Extraction (Phase 19.1)
49	
50	- [x] **SDK-01**: @cipherbox/core package contains all CipherBox domain types, metadata schemas, validators, metadata encrypt/decrypt, vault init, IPNS record utilities (extracted from crypto)
51	- [x] **SDK-02**: @cipherbox/crypto package contains only pure crypto primitives and key derivation (no domain-aware functions after cleanup)
52	- [x] **SDK-03**: @cipherbox/api-client generates typed HTTP functions from openapi.json without React dependencies, with configurable instance factory
53	- [x] **SDK-04**: @cipherbox/sdk-core provides stateless folder-aware operations with explicit parameter passing (no Zustand/browser deps)
54	- [x] **SDK-05**: sdk-core IPFS/IPNS functions accept SdkContext (apiUrl + getAccessToken) instead of reading browser globals
55	- [x] **SDK-06**: @cipherbox/sdk provides stateful CipherBoxClient class with internal folder tree, key cache, and event emission
56	- [x] **SDK-07**: SDK bin operations (add, restore, permanent delete, empty) and share operations (create, revoke) work through stateful client
57	- [x] **SDK-08**: Web app creates CipherBoxClient on vault load and destroys on logout; Zustand stores subscribe to SDK events
58	- [x] **SDK-09**: React hooks refactored to thin wrappers calling SDK client methods instead of service functions directly
59	- [x] **SDK-10**: All transitional re-exports removed from @cipherbox/crypto; domain type imports enforced at compile time
60	- [x] **SDK-11**: Release Please configured for independent per-package versioning
61	
62	### Rust SDK Extraction (Phase 23)
63	
64	- [x] **RSDK-01**: Cargo workspace at repo root with centralized `[workspace.dependencies]` and all five Rust crates as members
65	- [x] **RSDK-02**: `cipherbox-crypto` crate contains pure crypto primitives (AES-GCM/CTR, ECIES, Ed25519, HKDF, IPNS name derivation) with no domain knowledge
66	- [x] **RSDK-03**: Shared JSON test vectors in `tests/vectors/` loaded by both Rust and TypeScript test suites for cross-language parity verification
67	- [x] **RSDK-04**: `cipherbox-core` crate contains domain types (FolderMetadata, FileMetadata, RecycleBinMetadata, DeviceRegistry), metadata encrypt/decrypt, vault blob v2, IPNS record creation
68	- [x] **RSDK-05**: `cipherbox-api-client` crate provides typed HTTP client for all CipherBox API endpoints used by desktop
69	- [x] **RSDK-06**: `cipherbox-fuse` crate contains platform-agnostic FUSE abstractions (InodeTable, caches, file handles) and platform-specific modules behind feature flags (fuse for macOS/Linux, winfsp for Windows)
70	- [x] **RSDK-07**: `cipherbox-sdk` crate contains stateful orchestration (SyncDaemon, WriteQueue, KeyState, device registry ops) with no Tauri dependency
71	- [x] **RSDK-08**: Desktop app is a thin Tauri shell (commands/, tray/, main.rs) with all logic delegated to workspace crates
72	- [x] **RSDK-09**: CI workflows use workspace-level cargo commands, cache root Cargo.lock, and include cross-language vector parity gate
73	- [x] **RSDK-10**: Release Please configured for independent versioning of all five Rust crates
74	
75	### Bug Fixes & Test Infrastructure (Phase 24)
76	
77	- [x] **BUGFIX-01**: Bin IPNS name resolves correctly (no 404 errors on recycle bin operations)
78	- [x] **BUGFIX-02**: Device registry parses without crypto format errors
79	- [x] **TEST-01**: Headless Node.js load tests call sdk-core functions directly without Playwright browser overhead
80	- [x] **TEST-02**: Vault v2 recovery tool has automated E2E test coverage
81	- [x] **TEST-03**: Load tests handle 401 responses with automatic token refresh
82	
83	### Desktop Enhancements (Phase 25)
84	
85	- [x] **DESKTOP-01**: Desktop app checks for and installs updates (Tauri updater or custom mechanism)
86	- [x] **DESKTOP-02**: Files created via FUSE mount are enrolled with TEE for automatic IPNS republishing
87	
88	### Observability & UX Tuning (Phase 26)
89	
90	- [x] **OBS-01**: Grafana alerts fire when IPNS/IPFS/API response times exceed p95 thresholds from Phase 18/22 baselines
91	- [x] **OBS-02**: Client-side timeouts and retry config tuned for sub-2s perceived latency on common operations
92	
93	### Writable Shares PoC (Phase 27)
94	
95	- [x] **SHARE-01**: Share entity has `permission` column (`'read' | 'write'`, default `'read'`) and `encrypted_ipns_key` column (nullable bytea) with idempotent migration
96	- [x] **SHARE-02**: CreateShareDto and response DTOs include `permission` and `encryptedIpnsKey` fields; API client regenerated
97	- [x] **SHARE-03**: IPNS publish endpoint authorizes write-share recipients (checks shares table for active write share when userId does not match FolderIpns owner)
98	- [x] **SHARE-04**: Owner can upgrade (read->write) or downgrade (write->read) a share's permission via PATCH /shares/:shareId/permission
99	- [x] **SHARE-05**: Share store types (ReceivedShare, SentShare) include `permission` field; ReceivedShare includes `encryptedIpnsKey`
100	- [x] **SHARE-06**: ShareDialog has terminal-style permission toggle ([ READ-ONLY ] / [ READ-WRITE ]) with IPNS key wrapping for write shares
101	- [x] **SHARE-07**: ShareDialog recipients list shows per-recipient permission label with inline upgrade/downgrade controls
102	- [x] **SHARE-08**: SharedFileBrowser shows [RW] badge (green) for write shares and [RO] badge (dim) for read-only shares
103	- [x] **SHARE-09**: Write-share recipients see upload/mkdir toolbar and full context menu (rename, delete) in shared folders
104	- [x] **SHARE-10**: Write operations in shared folders use unwrapped IPNS key with 30s sync polling and withConflictRetry for multi-writer coordination
105	
106	## v1.2 Requirements
107	
108	Deferred to future release. Tracked but not in current roadmap.
109	
110	### IPNS Enhancements
111	
112	- **IPNS-05**: CRDT-based share discovery via IPNS inbox (replace server-side shares table)
113	- **IPNS-06**: folder_ipns CID cache made advisory (IPNS becomes primary source)
114	
115	### Database Minimization
116	
117	- **DB-01**: Device registry approval workflow migrated off database
118	- **DB-02**: pinned_cids table eliminated (alternative quota tracking via IPFS MFS or client-reported)
119	
120	### BYO-IPFS Advanced
121	
122	- **BYO-08**: Client-direct IPFS upload mode (bypass server relay for power users)
123	
124	## Out of Scope
125	
126	Explicitly excluded. Documented to prevent scope creep.
127	
128	| Feature                             | Reason                                                                                                                                |
129	| ----------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
130	| Full database elimination (zero DB) | Auth tables (users, auth_methods, refresh_tokens) require indexed queryable storage. IPFS is not a database.                          |
131	| IPNS PubSub as primary resolution   | Only works when publisher and resolver share PubSub peers. Not persistent. Doesn't scale to thousands of IPNS names per user.         |
132	| CRDT for all metadata               | Premature -- optimistic concurrency already solves folder conflicts. CRDTs add state size growth and cross-platform merge complexity. |
133	| DNSLink as IPNS alternative         | Requires DNS infrastructure per user. Propagation is slow. Doesn't support per-folder/per-file IPNS model.                            |
134	| Share migration to IPFS             | Requires CRDT inbox protocol (research-only this milestone). Complex query patterns (filter by recipient, status, revocation).        |
135	| Encrypted Productivity Suite        | Deferred to Milestone 4 (v2.0) -- billing, teams, doc editors, signing, AWS Nitro TEE                                                 |
136	| Mobile apps                         | Deferred to Milestone 4+                                                                                                              |
137

132	3. Share create (read/write) → recipient access → write-share ops with conflict retry
133	4. Desktop FUSE create → TEE enrollment → async FilePointer resolution
134	5. SDK layering clean both stacks (TS: sdk → sdk-core → api-client/core/crypto; Rust crates equivalent) — no violations
135	6. Client + API metrics → Prometheus (Alloy scrape incl. Kubo + Someguy) → Grafana dashboards → alerts

- **Key decisions:** D-04..D-10 per-package versioning strategy; D-15..D-20 PR-time auto-analysis with manual overrides; D-25..D-30 post-merge `release-as` injection with concurrency serialization; D-31..D-33 batched GitHub releases with component tags; D-34..D-36 date-based staging with conditional skipping patterns (phase 1 only).
- **Deferred/tech-debt:** Smart dispatch for staging (conditional job skipping); spec-anchored API versioning; coordinated 1.0 milestone.

### Phase 42 — API unpin integrity
- **One-liner:** Closed the unpin ownership gap by verifying caller owns a pinned_cid row before unpinning, reference-counting CIDs across users, and transactionally decrementing quota via a pending-unpins outbox with BullMQ retry.
- **Status:** passed
- **Accomplishments:** Added `PendingUnpin` entity and pending_unpins table with unique CID index; created idx_pinned_cids_cid for O(log n) refcount queries; wired `guardedUnpin` into the unpin endpoint with per-CID advisory locks; implemented outbox pattern with `recordUnpin` integration and graceful Kubo "not pinned" handling; added metrics for cross-user audit and drift detection; developed backfill script for non-BYO quota repair.
- **Key decisions:** D-01 silent 2XX on no-row calls (no existence oracle); D-03 row-first, Kubo best-effort; D-04 per-CID advisory xact lock for race closure; D-05 outbox pattern with transactional insert and post-commit Kubo attempt; D-07 refcount across all users equally (no BYO special case).
- **Deferred/tech-debt:** Wire `provider.unpin` into BYO client delete flows; writable-share version-prune leak (pre-existing); upload/unpin race hardening (detect only, not close).

### Phase 43 — FUSE write durability
- **One-liner:** Made FUSE writes durable with a persisted out-of-callback journal and crash-recovery replay, fixing silent data loss on release() and mkdir parent-publish conflicts that orphaned child folders.
- **Status:** passed
- **Accomplishments:** Built persist-backed `WriteQueue` with fsync barrier before reply; implemented `JournalOp` enum covering `UploadFile` and `MkdirPublish` with stable IPNS identifiers (no ino/parent_ino); added vault-scoped entry loading and park-on-max-retries; wired journal fsync before `reply.ok()/reply.entry()` on both fuser and WinFsp; implemented crash-recovery replay with proper key unwrapping and CAS publish; added `SyncDaemon` integration for pending/failed counts and write-parked notifications.
- **Key decisions:** D-01 journal in `crates/sdk`; D-02 stable identifiers; D-03 both UploadFile and MkdirPublish ops; D-04 fsync barrier before reply; D-05 ciphertext+wrapped keys only (never plaintext); D-06 merge-on-409 replay from fresh remote; D-09 park-on-exhaustion (never drop); D-11 live-session re-arm + debounced retry.
- **Deferred/tech-debt:** Full pending-uploads UI (desktop window with retry/export); Rust FUSE 409-merge parity (phase 43 replay does upsert-into-fresh but live-session rebuild approximates).

### Phase 44 — IPNS conflict handling
- **One-liner:** Implemented three-way merge conflict resolution for concurrent IPNS writes with a generic `publishWithCas` engine and an optional `baseChildren` snapshot for detecting deletes vs adds.
- **Status:** passed
- **Accomplishments:** Created `ConflictError` class with ipnsName/attempts/lastRemoteSeq; implemented `mergeChildren` pure function covering edit-beats-delete, local-add, remote-add, and union-fallback semantics; wired into both `updateFolderMetadataAndPublish` and `updateFileMetadata` with 4-attempt retry budget + exponential backoff; extended file record CAS and version merging with `prunedCids` deduplication.
- **Key decisions:** D-01 three-way merge (base/local/remote); D-02 union fallback when no baseChildren; D-03 re-encrypt before republishing; D-04 4 attempts with backoff; D-06 extend CAS to file records; D-07 loser-becomes-version for file conflicts with version cap from vault settings.
- **Deferred/tech-debt:** Rust FUSE 409-merge parity (live-session rebuild approximates op-replay but can stomp remote-only changes); full CRDT model (explicitly deferred to inbox research).

### Phase 45 — Desktop FUSE write-durability cleanup
- **One-liner:** Paid down write-journal structural debt with shared journal-entry builders, typed IPNS-resolve outcome, option-string serde compat shim, and added six safety-net tests for crash-recovery and replay paths.
- **Status:** passed
- **Accomplishments:** Consolidated fuser/winfsp journal entry builders into `journal_helpers.rs`; added `IpnsResolveOutcome` enum replacing substring matching in replay; implemented `deser_opt_string` serde helper mapping legacy empty strings to None; memoized `resolve_folder_key` cache in replay_for_vault; wired `publish_file_metadata` reuse in replay_upload_entry; created shared `default_journal_dir()` and `JOURNAL_MAX_RETRIES` helpers; added 6 test functions covering crash-mid-write, partial-journal, retry-exhaustion, replay-no-touch-failed, cache-memoization, and merge ordering.
- **Key decisions:** #11 consolidate journal builders; #12 shared helpers; #14 test coverage; #15 cache memoization; #18 `Option<String>` sentinel; #19 typed resolve outcome; #20 publish_file_metadata reuse.
- **Deferred/tech-debt:** None — pure hygiene/test phase (behavior changes already landed in prior phases).

### Phase 46 — Desktop FUSE data-loss bugs + replay hardening
- **One-liner:** Closed three data-loss bugs (mkdir orphan on conflict, release() false durability, stale-mount crash recovery) and hardened replay with legacy-entry parking and strict IPNS resolve for transient failures.
- **Status:** passed
- **Accomplishments:** Added Linux stale-mount auto-recovery via `/proc/self/mountinfo` parsing and lazy fusermount3 unmount; wired parent-publish durability retry logic and journaled parent IPNS key for mkdir conflict upsert; added early-return park-guard for legacy empty `file_meta_ipns_name` entries; implemented strict cache-bypassing IPNS resolve in replay path; created ReplySender export and test-support harness with CaptureSender; added characterization tests for mkdir-conflict re-arm, release durability, and journal-entry builders.
- **Key decisions:** REQ-1/2 behavior pre-landed (PR #487/#491), phase adds tests; REQ-3 new Linux-only recovery path with EEXIST retry; REQ-4 park legacy entries; REQ-5 strict resolve on transient failures; REQ-6 handler test harness.
- **Deferred/tech-debt:** None — live UAT for Linux stale-mount signed off by maintainer on 2026-06-15.

### Phase 47 — SDK folder-state publish consolidation
- **One-liner:** Unified folder-state ownership in SDK client, consolidated file/folder CAS-retry into one `publishWithCas` helper, and simplified web hooks to route through SDK-owned methods with projection-only folder stores.
- **Status:** passed
- **Accomplishments:** Created generic `publishWithCas<TData>` helper with resolve→encrypt→upload→CAS→409→merge→ConflictError skeleton for both file and folder; delegated `updateFolderMetadataAndPublish` and `updateFileMetadata` to publishWithCas with baseChildren snapshot encapsulation; added `replaceFile`/`restoreFileVersion`/`deleteFileVersion` client methods with folderTree management; rewired web hooks (`useFileOperations`, `useFileVersions`) to route through SDK client and drop redundant `updatedChildren` returns; made `useFolderStore` projection-only via `subscribeToSdk` folder:updated/loaded handlers; deleted `reconcileFolderState` and the `ensureFolderRegistered` seeding band-aid.
- **Key decisions:** P01 one publishWithCas owner; P02 drop updatedChildren returns; unpin prunedCids in shared-write with fire-and-forget; P03 client methods own publish+sequence+emit; P04 hooks route through SDK only; P05 folder.store projection-only via SDK events.
- **Deferred/tech-debt:** None — prior planning-time TC08 resurrection race now covered by deterministic tests.

### Phase 48 — SDK self-bootstrap regression fix and shared-folder metadata
- **One-liner:** Restored green main after self-bootstrap regression by reconciling on IPNS sequence number, deleted redundant folder seeding, extended single-ownership to shared-folder writes, and closed Phase 14's plaintext itemName leak with ECIES-encrypted at-rest ciphertext and lazy backfill persist.
- **Status:** passed
- **Accomplishments:** Fixed `loadFolder` reconciliation guard (keep fresher in-memory state instead of clobbering); deleted all `ensureFolderRegistered` seed call sites and the function definition; added `sharedFolderTree` sibling state keyed by shareId with key-zeroing on delete; created shared-write methods with `publishWithCas` delegation and `sharedFolder:updated` emission; added `itemNameEncrypted` column to shares + share_invites tables; implemented ECIES wrap on send and decrypt on receive; added `PATCH /shares/:shareId/item-name` endpoint for sharer-authorized lazy backfill persist; wired `backfillSentShareItemNames` to call the persist endpoint.
- **Key decisions:** REQ-1 reconcile on sequence before write-back; REQ-2 delete seeding; REQ-3 sibling sharedFolderTree; shared methods own publish+emit; REQ-4 at-rest encryption + lazy backfill persist.
- **Deferred/tech-debt:** None — live UAT signed off by maintainer on 2026-06-18 (E2E 27766162738 green).

### Phase 49 — Shared-folder move intra-share and useFolderNavigation consolidation
- **One-liner:** Enabled write-permission share recipients to move files between subfolders with automatic FileMetadata re-encryption, consolidated ECIES unwrap onto SDK, and brought shared-view move UX to parity with private vault (batch + drag).
- **Status:** passed
- **Accomplishments:** Implemented `moveInSharedFolder` stateless op with DEST-first publish, re-keying FileMetadata from source to destination folderKey, then SOURCE publish; created `enumerateSharedSubtree` DFS with writable-flag detection and cycle guard; consolidated web-side key unwrap in `useFolderNavigation` onto SDK `ensureFolderLoaded`; added right-click context-menu move + `SharedMoveDialog` for subtree picker with disabled-state for read-only folders; added multi-select batch move via `SelectionActionBar` + `SharedMoveDialog` with per-item items prop; added drag-and-drop to folder rows with application/json payload routing; authored `shared-folder-move.spec.ts` e2e proving decrypt-survival for recipient (test 4.3) and owner (test 5.1) after cross-client sync.
- **Key decisions:** Intra-share moves only (single shared folder); DEST-first publish with re-key + SOURCE publish; recipient must have write key for destination; collision and write-cap validated per-item; selection preserved on failure; decrypt-survival e2e authored + executed (green CI run 27766162738).
- **Deferred/tech-debt:** None — live UAT signed off by maintainer on 2026-06-18 (e2e green + functional routing verified).

Project Overview\n\n**CipherBox** is a production-grade, privacy-first encrypted cloud storage platform built on IPFS/IPNS and Web3Auth. Files are encrypted client-side before they leave the device, and encryption keys live only in client memory — the server is *cryptographically unable* to read user data (zero-knowledge relay).\n\nBy the start of v1.1 (post-v1.0, shipped 2026-03-05) the product already had: Web3Auth MPC auth, AES-256-GCM + ECIES encryption, IPFS/IPNS storage, full file/folder CRUD, user-to-user and link sharing, client-side search, MFA, file versioning, recycle bin, and cross-platform desktop apps (macOS/Windows/Linux via Tauri + FUSE/WinFsp).\n\n**v1.1 goal:** make CipherBox more *IPFS-native* and operationally mature. Four headline thrusts:\n\n1. **Reliable IPNS resolution** — replace the flaky `delegated-ipfs.dev` dependency.\n2. **Database minimization** — move vault crypto material (`rootFolderKey`) out of the DB into the IPFS vault blob, so the server holds no key material at all.\n3. **Bring-your-own IPFS** — let users point at their own IPFS node via a server-relay flow.\n4. **Performance baselines & observability** — instrument the whole stack (API, client, IPFS/IPNS, TEE) with Prometheus/Grafana/Faro and document capacity.\n\nBeyond the original four, the milestone absorbed a large body of **architecture and reliability work**: a five-package TypeScript SDK split (mirrored by five Rust crates), writable shares, FUSE write-durability hardening, the production Phala TEE migration, per-package release engineering, and a long tail of correctness fixes around IPNS conflict handling, unpin integrity, and shared-folder state.\n\nThis was a large milestone: **222 commits, ~2,100 files touched (+304.7k / −50.5k LOC), spanning 2026-03-07 → 2026-06-18 (~3.5 months).**\n\n---\n\n## 2. Architecture & Technical Decisions\n\nThe defining architectural move of v1.1 is a **layered SDK extracted out of the web app**, in both TypeScript and Rust, so that domain logic is framework-agnostic, testable headlessly, and shareable across web + desktop.\n\n### TypeScript SDK (Phase 19.1, refined through 31/38/47/48/49)\n\nFive packages, strict dependency direction `sdk → sdk-core → api-client / core / crypto`:\n\n- **`@cipherbox/crypto`** — pure primitives only (AES-GCM/CTR, ECIES, Ed25519, HKDF, IPNS name derivation). No domain knowledge.\n- **`@cipherbox/core`** — domain types, metadata schemas, validators, metadata encrypt/decrypt, vault blob v2, IPNS record utils.\n- **`@cipherbox/api-client`** — typed HTTP client generated from `openapi.json`, no React deps.\n- **`@cipherbox/sdk-core`** — *stateless* folder/file/IPFS/IPNS operations taking an explicit `SdkContext` (apiUrl + token getter). Built for headless load testing.\n- **`@cipherbox/sdk`** — *stateful* `CipherBoxClient` owning the folder tree, key cache, and event emission. Zustand stores subscribe to SDK events (no component refactor).\n\n- **Decision:** SDK-first decomposition — move framework-agnostic logic *down* into packages, leave React hooks as thin wrappers.\n  - **Why:** Enables headless Node load tests, multi-platform reuse, and a single source of truth for folder state.\n  - **Phases:** 19.1 (split), 31 (decompose hooks), 38 (retire `folder.service.ts`/`bin.service.ts`, −2,030 LOC), 47/48 (single folder-state owner).\n\n### Rust SDK (Phase 23)\n\nFive crates mirroring the TS hierarchy — `cipherbox-crypto`, `cipherbox-core`, `cipherbox-api-client`, `cipherbox-fuse`, `cipherbox-sdk` — in a root Cargo workspace. The desktop app becomes a thin Tauri shell.\n\n- **Decision:** Cross-language JSON test vectors in `tests/vectors/`, with a CI parity gate that fails the build if TS and Rust crypto outputs diverge.\n  - **Why:** Guarantees byte-for-byte interop between web and desktop crypto.\n  - **Phase:** 23 (closed out 2026-06-18 after Windows WinFsp ops migrated in 25/33).\n\n### Zero-knowledge vault (Phase 20)\n\n- **Decision:** Vault blob **v2** — `rootFolderKey` is ECIES-wrapped *inside* the IPFS blob header; the DB crypto columns (`encryptedRootFolderKey`, `encryptedRootIpnsPrivateKey`) are dropped entirely.\n  - **Why:** Server holds zero key material; `encryptedRootIpnsPrivateKey` is HKDF-derivable so it's redundant.\n  - **Outcome:** Exceeded the original requirement — the DB read-fallback was removed completely, not just deprecated.\n\n### IPNS reliability (Phase 19)\n\n- **Decision:** Self-host **Someguy** (v0.11.1, standard DHT mode) as a delegated-routing sidecar, replacing `delegated-ipfs.dev`.\n  - **Why:** Eliminate an unreliable third-party dependency; standard (not accelerated) DHT fits the 768 MB staging VPS.\n\n### BYO-IPFS (Phase 21)\n\n- **Decision:** Client-direct pinning via a `PinningProvider` abstraction (Kubo / PSA / Pinata) with a `DualPinProvider` (primary must-succeed, secondary best-effort). **All IPNS publishes still route through the CipherBox API** regardless of BYO config.\n  - **Why:** Data sovereignty for users while keeping IPNS sequence/conflict control centralized. Credentials are ECIES-encrypted with the TEE public key; the connection test is TEE-routed with SSRF validation.\n\n### Other foundational decisions\n\n- **Concurrent upload pipeline** (19.2, 37): `Promise.allSettled` pin orchestration + Kubo `pebbleds` datastore (−13% p95); parallel encrypt+pin with `p-limit(3)` and a Web Worker for zero-copy encryption, collapsing O(N) IPNS publishes to O(1).\n- **IPNS conflict handling** (44, 47): a generic `publishWithCas` engine with three-way `mergeChildren` (base/local/remote), 4-attempt backoff, and \"loser-becomes-version\" for file conflicts.\n- **Unpin integrity** (42): ownership-guarded unpin with cross-user CID reference counting and a transactional pending-unpins outbox (BullMQ retry).\n- **FUSE write durability** (43/45/46): an fsync'd ciphertext write journal with crash-recovery replay; never drops a write (parks on retry exhaustion).\n- **Observability stack:** Prometheus histograms (`cipherbox_ipfs_ipns_duration_seconds`, `cipherbox_tee_*`, republish batch) → Alloy scrape (incl. Kubo + Someguy) → Grafana dashboards + 17 alert rules; Grafana **Faro** for web RUM with strict privacy scrubbing (no session replay, publicKey-only identity).\n- **Per-package release engineering** (41): 15 independently-versioned packages, conventional-commit analysis at PR time, date-based staging tags.\n\n---\n\n## 3. full pending-uploads desktop UI deferred.\n- **Phase 42:** Wire `provider.unpin` into BYO client delete flows; writable-share version-prune leak (pre-existing); upload/unpin race is detect-only.\n- **Phase 37:** Adaptive concurrency by file size, AbortSignal cancellation, dual-pin secondary-pin warning events.\n\n**Explicitly deferred to v1.2+ (out of scope by design):** CRDT-based IPNS inbox for share discovery, `folder_ipns` cache made advisory, device-registry off DB, `pinned_cids` elimination, client-direct IPFS upload (bypass relay). Mobile apps, real-time collab, and the Encrypted Productivity Suite remain Milestone 4 (v2.0).\n\n---\n\n## 7. Getting Started\n\n**Run the project (local dev):**\n\n```bash\ndocker compose -f docker/docker-compose.yml up -d   # Kubo, Postgres, Someguy, etc.\npnpm --filter @cipherbox/api dev                     # NestJS API\npnpm --filter @cipherbox/web dev                      # React web app (Vite, :5173)\n```\n\nSee `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` for full environment setup.\n\n**Regenerate the API client after API changes** (enforced by a pre-commit hook):\n\n```bash\npnpm api:generate\n```\n\n**Key directories:**\n\n- `apps/api` — NestJS backend (zero-knowledge relay, IPNS publish, shares, quota)\n- `apps/web` — React 18 web app (Zustand stores subscribe to SDK events)\n- `apps/desktop` — Tauri shell; logic delegated to Rust crates\n- `apps/tee-worker` — TEE republisher (Phala Cloud CVM in prod)\n- `packages/{crypto,core,api-client,sdk-core,sdk}` — the TypeScript SDK (dependency order: sdk → sdk-core → api-client/core/crypto)\n- `crates/{crypto,core,api-client,fuse,sdk}` — the Rust SDK mirror\n- `tests/vectors/` — cross-language crypto test vectors (parity-gated in CI)\n- `docs/` — single source of truth (ARCHITECTURE, FILESYSTEM_SPECIFICATION, METADATA_SCHEMAS, CAPACITY, VAULT_EXPORT_FORMAT, …)\n\n**Where to look first:**\n\n- Folder/file operations → `packages/sdk-core` (stateless ops) + `packages/sdk` `CipherBoxClient` (stateful owner of folder tree).\n- Crypto → `packages/crypto` (primitives) / `packages/core` (vault blob v2, metadata, IPNS records).\n- IPNS conflict logic → `publishWithCas` + `mergeChildren` in `packages/sdk-core`.\n- Desktop FUSE → `crates/fuse` (platform modules behind feature flags) + write journal in `crates/sdk`.\n\n**Tests:**\n\n- Web/SDK unit: `pnpm --filter <pkg> test` (vitest — note web `include` is `src/**/*.test.ts` only).\n- Rust: workspace-level `cargo test` (includes cross-language vector parity gate).\n- E2E: Playwright suites in `apps/web` (web-e2e gate runs on main push); desktop E2E script pairs.\n- SDK load: headless vitest harness in `tests/load/` (needs local Docker stack + API).\n\n---\n\n## Stats\n\n- **Timeline:** 2026-03-07 → 2026-06-18 (~3.5 months)\n- **Phases:** 34 / 34 complete (151 / 151 plans; avg ~5.5 min/plan, ~16.5h total execution)\n- **Commits:** 222 (in milestone range, inclusive of start commit)\n- **Files changed:** 2,109 (+304,729 / −50,510)\n- **Contributors:** Michael Yankelev (173 commits), cipherbox-release-bot[bot] (44), dependabot[bot] (5)\n- **Requirements:** 66 / 66 satisfied\n- **Audit (2026-06-11):** `gaps_found` → process gaps closed by ledger close-out; milestone now 100% complete\n"

---

# Milestone v1.1 — IPFS Infrastructure (COMPLETE)

**34 phases · 151 plans · 222 commits · ~2,100 files (+304.7k / −50.5k) · 2026-03-07 → 2026-06-18**

## What it was
Make CipherBox more **IPFS-native** and operationally mature, on top of the zero-knowledge v1.0 base. Four headline thrusts — reliable IPNS resolution, database minimization (no key material server-side), bring-your-own-IPFS, and a full performance/observability baseline — plus a large absorbed body of SDK-architecture and FUSE-reliability work.

## The defining architectural move
A **layered SDK extracted out of the web app, mirrored in Rust**:

- **TS:** `sdk → sdk-core → api-client / core / crypto` (Phase 19.1; refined in 31/38/47/48). Stateless `sdk-core` (headless-testable) + stateful `CipherBoxClient` owning folder tree; Zustand stores subscribe to SDK events.
- **Rust:** five crates mirroring the hierarchy (Phase 23), desktop reduced to a thin Tauri shell, with **cross-language test vectors parity-gated in CI**.

## Headline deliverables by workstream
| Workstream | Phases | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Zero-knowledge vault | 20 | `rootFolderKey` ECIES-wrapped in **IPFS blob v2**; DB crypto columns **dropped entirely** |
| IPNS reliability | 19 | Self-hosted **Someguy** replaces delegated-ipfs.dev |
| BYO-IPFS | 21 | Kubo/PSA/Pinata providers, dual-pin, TEE-routed connection test |
| Upload perf | 19.2, 36, 37 | Concurrent pins + pebbleds (−13% p95); parallel encrypt+pin pipeline, Web Worker, O(N)→O(1) publishes |
| Observability | 18, 22, 26, 30 | Prometheus histograms → Grafana (17 alerts) + Faro RUM; `CAPACITY.md` |
| Writable shares | 27, 48, 49 | Full CRUD for write-recipients; ECIES-encrypted shared item names; intra-share move with re-key |
| FUSE durability | 32/33, 43/45/46 | Async FilePointer resolution; fsync'd write journal + crash replay; 3 data-loss bugs closed |
| Correctness | 42, 44, 47 | Ownership-guarded unpin + refcount outbox; three-way `publishWithCas` merge |
| TEE / release eng | 35, 41 | Production Phala CVM migration; per-package semver with PR-time commit analysis |

## Requirements: 66 / 66 satisfied
All IPNS, VAULT, BYO, PERF, SDK, RSDK, DESKTOP, OBS, SHARE requirements mapped and met.

> **Honest caveat:** the 2026-06-11 audit returned `gaps_found` — 4 PERF reqs "orphaned" and phases 18/31/32 lacking `VERIFICATION.md`. These were **process gaps, not code gaps**, and were closed by the verification-ledger close-out (#512, #513). STATE.md now reports 100% complete.

## Notable carried tech debt
- Rust FUSE 409-merge **approximates** TS op-replay (can stomp remote-only changes) — 43/44.
- BYO `provider.unpin` not yet wired into client delete flows; writable-share version-prune leak — 42.
- Desktop platform code-signing, pending-uploads UI, Windows mkdir-publish-retry TODO — 25/43/23.
- Deferred to v1.2+: CRDT IPNS inbox, advisory `folder_ipns` cache, client-direct upload.

1	---
2	created: 2026-06-17T00:00:00.000Z
3	title: Shared-folder move (when built) must re-encrypt file metadata to the destination folderKey
4	area: feature
5	severity: medium
6	source: decrypt-fail-after-move debug session (PR fix/decrypt-fail-after-move)
7	files:
8	  - apps/web/src/hooks/useSharedWriteOps.ts
9	  - apps/web/src/components/file-browser/SharedFileBrowser.tsx
10	  - packages/sdk/src/share/shared-write.ts
11	  - packages/sdk/src/client.ts
12	---
13	
14	## Problem
15	
16	Moving a file between folders within a shared folder is **not implemented today**:
17	
18	- `SharedFileBrowser.tsx` renders the `ContextMenu` with `onRename`/`onDelete`
19	  only (gated by `isWritable`) — there is no `onMove`.
20	- `useSharedWriteOps` exposes `uploadFile`, `createFolder`, `renameItem`,
21	  `deleteItem`, `updateSharedFile` — no move operation.
22	- `CipherBoxClient.moveItem` operates on the owner's `folderTree`, not the
23	  `sharedFolderTree`.
24	
25	So a write-permission recipient cannot currently move files around inside a share
26	(a reasonable capability they would expect).
27	
28	## Why this is captured here
29	
30	The `decrypt-fail-after-move` fix established that a file's `FileMetadata` IPNS
31	record is AES-256-GCM encrypted with its **parent folder's folderKey**. Any
32	operation that re-parents a file to a folder with a different `folderKey` MUST
33	re-encrypt the record (resolve with the source key, re-publish with the
34	destination key, `createVersion: false`) or every later preview/edit/download
35	fails with `CryptoError: Decryption failed`. This was fixed for:
36	
37	- private-vault move (`CipherBoxClient.moveItem`)
38	- bin restore to a different folder (`restoreFromBin`, using the folderKey
39	  captured on the `BinEntry` at delete time)
40	
41	## When implementing shared-folder move
42	
43	Add a shared move (e.g. `moveInSharedFolder` in `shared-write.ts` + an SDK client
44	method that owns the `sharedFolderTree` publish/sequence bookkeeping, wired through
45	`useSharedWriteOps` and an `onMove` in the shared `ContextMenu`). It **must** apply
46	the same re-encrypt step when the source and destination subfolders have different
47	`folderKey`s — within a share, each subfolder still has its own key. Add an e2e
48	that asserts decrypted content survives a within-share move (mirroring
49	`tests/web-e2e/tests/move-restore-content.spec.ts`).
50	
```

35	
36	# Phase 42: Code Review Report
37	
38	**Reviewed:** 2026-06-12
39	**Depth:** standard
40	**Files Reviewed:** 21
41	**Status:** issues_found
42	
43	## Narrative Findings (AI reviewer)
44	
45	## Summary
46	
47	Reviewed the guarded-unpin implementation: `guardedUnpin` in `vault.service.ts`, the controller rewiring, the `pending_unpins` outbox + BullMQ drain/drift processor, two migrations, the non-BYO backfill script, the cross-user audit metric/alert, and the spec files.
48	
49	The core security properties hold under adversarial tracing:
50	
51	- Ownership is enforced (delete is scoped to `{userId, cid}`); the silent-2XX path is response-identical for unknown vs cross-user CIDs (no oracle).
52	- The advisory lock is taken before any read/write, and refcounting under READ COMMITTED correctly serializes concurrent unpins of the same CID.
53	- A share recipient who learns a victim's CID and registers it (BYO) cannot trigger a physical unpin of the victim's data — the refcount sees the victim's row.
54	- `pinned_cids` has `@Unique(['userId', 'cid'])`, so refcount inflation via duplicate rows is not possible.
55	- `openapi.json` matches the opaque `UnpinResponseDto { success: boolean }` shape; the Grafana alert metric name matches `cipherbox_unpin_cross_user_attempts_total` in `metrics.service.ts`, and the file follows the same provisioning conventions (placeholder UIDs, `noDataState`/`execErrState`) as sibling alert files. JSON is structurally valid.
56	
57	No critical findings. Seven warnings: a latent SQL overflow in the advisory-lock hash, an upload-compensation path that leaks Kubo pins and pollutes the cross-user security metric, a stale-outbox race the D-13 rationale does not cover, a counter-vs-gauge metric defect, a TOCTOU window in the destructive backfill script, an illusory defensive predicate in that same script, and an unbounded-retention consequence of counting BYO advisory rows in the hosted refcount.
58	
59	## Warnings
60	
61	### WR-01: `abs(hashtext(...))` can raise "integer out of range"; the comment claims to fix a pitfall it actually introduces
62	
63	**File:** `apps/api/src/vault/vault.service.ts:255`
64	**Issue:** `SELECT abs(hashtext($1))::bigint AS h` applies `abs()` to the `int4` result of `hashtext()` BEFORE casting. For any CID whose `hashtext` is exactly `-2147483648` (INT_MIN), `abs(int4)` raises `ERROR: integer out of range` — deterministically, every time, for that CID. That file would become permanently undeletable via the API (500 on every unpin) and its quota row permanently stuck. Probability per CID is ~2^-32, but the failure is permanent once it occurs. The comment ("abs() avoids bigint-out-of-range on negative hashtext values") is backwards: `pg_advisory_xact_lock` accepts a signed `bigint`, so negative values were never a problem — `abs()` is the only failure mode here.
65	**Fix:**
66	
67	```sql
68	SELECT hashtext($1)::bigint AS h
69	```
70	
71	Drop `abs()` entirely (negative lock keys are valid), or if a non-negative key is desired, cast first: `abs(hashtext($1)::bigint)`.
72	
73	### WR-02: Upload compensation via `guardedUnpin` is a no-op that leaks Kubo pins and fires the cross-user security alert on internal failures
74	
75	**File:** `apps/api/src/ipfs/ipfs.controller.ts:119-128` (interacting with `apps/api/src/vault/vault.service.ts:261-268`)
76	**Issue:** When `recordPin` fails after a successful Kubo pin, the compensation call `guardedUnpin(req.user.id, result.cid)` finds no `pinned_cids` row for the caller (the insert is exactly what failed), so it returns before any Kubo interaction. Two consequences:
77	
78	1. The just-created Kubo pin is never removed — the previous compensation (direct `unpinFile`) did remove it. The drift report only *counts and logs* orphans; nothing ever cleans them, so the leak is permanent.
79	2. If any other user holds the same CID (dedupe), the cross-user lookup at `vault.service.ts:263-267` matches and increments `cipherbox_unpin_cross_user_attempts_total` — which directly fires the new Grafana security alert. An internal DB failure during upload would page ops with a "cross-tenant probe" signal, polluting the exact audit channel D-02 was built for.
80	
81	**Fix:** In the upload compensation path, call `this.ipfsProvider.unpinFile(result.cid)` directly only when the CID has no `pinned_cids` rows at all (or add a `guardedUnpin` internal variant that skips the cross-user telemetry and falls back to a refcount-checked physical unpin for the no-row case). At minimum, exclude this path from the cross-user counter so the security signal stays clean.
82	
83	### WR-03: Drain worker and post-commit unpin never re-check refcount — a stale outbox row can unpin a re-pinned CID; the D-13 "sub-second window" rationale does not cover this path
84	
85	**File:** `apps/api/src/ipfs/pending-unpin/pending-unpin.processor.ts:50-62` (also `apps/api/src/vault/vault.service.ts:297-304`)
86	**Issue:** `drainPendingUnpins` unpins every outbox CID unconditionally. If a CID lands in `pending_unpins` (refcount hit zero, inline Kubo call failed) and the same CID is subsequently re-pinned and recorded (re-upload of identical ciphertext, or a pin-migration flow that re-pins existing CIDs), the next drain pass removes the pin from Kubo while a live `pinned_cids` row references it — content then becomes eligible for Kubo GC: data loss. The D-13 comment in the controller argues the race is negligible because it requires identical ciphertext within a "sub-second window"; for the outbox path the window is 5 minutes minimum and unbounded while Kubo is down, so only the identical-ciphertext premise protects you — and migration flows re-pin existing CIDs by design.
87	**Fix:** In the drain loop, before calling `unpinFile`, check `pinned_cids` for the CID; if any row exists, delete the outbox row without unpinning (the entry is stale):
88	
89	```typescript
90	const refs = await this.pinnedCidRepository.count({ where: { cid: row.cid } });
91	if (refs > 0) {
92	  await this.pendingUnpinRepository.delete({ cid: row.cid });
93	  continue;
94	}
95	```
96	
97	Apply the same guard to the inline post-commit path in `guardedUnpin` if desired (lower value there — the window is genuinely short).
98	
99	### WR-04: `driftOrphanedPinsTotal` is a Counter re-incremented for the same orphans every hourly run
100	
101	**File:** `apps/api/src/ipfs/pending-unpin/pending-unpin.processor.ts:92-97` (declared at `apps/api/src/metrics/metrics.service.ts:184-188`)
102	**Issue:** Each hourly drift run increments the counter once per unaccounted pin. A constant set of N orphans grows the metric by N every hour forever, so the value does not represent "total orphans" and any rate/threshold alert on it stays hot indefinitely even when drift is stable. The companion `pendingUnpinsGauge` correctly uses a Gauge with `.set()`; this metric measures the same kind of point-in-time state.
103	**Fix:** Replace with a Gauge set once per run:
104	
105	```typescript
106	let orphans = 0;
107	for (const cid of kuboPins) {
108	  if (!dbCids.has(cid)) {
109	    orphans++;
110	    this.logger.warn(`Drift: unaccounted Kubo pin cid=${cid}`);
111	  }
112	}
113	this.metricsService.driftOrphanedPinsGauge.set(orphans);
114	```
115	
116	### WR-05: Backfill script snapshots Kubo before querying the DB — rows for uploads that land in between are deleted as "phantoms"
117	
118	**File:** `scripts/backfill-pinned-cids.ts:85-136`
119	**Issue:** The script fetches the Kubo pin set (step 1), then queries `pinned_cids` (step 2). Any upload that completes between the two reads produces a row whose CID is absent from the stale Kubo snapshot — `selectRowsToDelete` classifies it as a phantom and the live-mode run deletes it. The user keeps the pin (Kubo) but loses the quota row permanently: quota under-count plus a perpetual drift-report orphan. Nothing in the script restricts candidates by age, and nothing in the file warns that it must run with the API stopped.
120	**Fix:** Add an age cutoff to the candidate query so in-flight uploads are never candidates:
121	
122	```sql
123	WHERE v.is_byo_user = false
124	  AND pc.pinned_at < NOW() - INTERVAL '1 hour'
125	```
126	
127	(Swapping the fetch order — DB first, Kubo second — also closes the window, since `guardedUnpin` now deletes rows before unpinning; the cutoff is the simpler, more robust guard.)
128	
129	### WR-06: Backfill query hardcodes `false::boolean AS "isByoUser"`, making the documented defensive re-assert illusory
130	
131	**File:** `scripts/backfill-pinned-cids.ts:127-136` (comment at 124-125)
132	**Issue:** The comment states "selectRowsToDelete re-asserts isByoUser === false defensively," but the SELECT fabricates the column as a literal `false` instead of selecting `v.is_byo_user`. The helper's BYO exclusion (the D-09 safety property its unit tests verify) can therefore never reject anything: if the `WHERE v.is_byo_user = false` clause is ever loosened or mis-edited, BYO rows arrive at the destructive delete already stamped non-BYO. In a script whose failure mode is corrupting advisory quota, a defense layer that cannot fire is worse than none because the comment claims it exists.
133	**Fix:**
134	
135	```sql
136	v.is_byo_user AS "isByoUser"
137	```
138	
139	Select the real column so the predicate genuinely re-checks it.
140	
141	### WR-07: BYO advisory rows block physical unpin of hosted content indefinitely — retention/privacy consequence of D-07
142	
143	**File:** `apps/api/src/vault/vault.service.ts:274-292`
144	**Issue:** Refcounting counts all `pinned_cids` rows for the CID with no origin filtering (deliberate, per D-07). But BYO advisory rows describe pins on the user's *own* node, never on CipherBox's Kubo. A share recipient learns a file's CID by design (sharing is a v1.0 feature); a BYO recipient can register that CID via `/ipfs/register-cid` (passes the format regex, costs them nothing — their quota is advisory). When the owner later deletes the file, refcount stays ≥ 1 and the ciphertext remains pinned on CipherBox's hosted Kubo indefinitely — the owner believes it deleted, and no process ever revisits the decision (drift report won't flag it: the CID is accounted for in `pinned_cids`). This conflates two pin namespaces (hosted Kubo vs. user nodes) inside one refcount and creates an unbounded server-side retention path controllable by a non-owner.
145	**Fix:** Count only rows attributable to hosted pinning in the physical-unpin decision, e.g. join to `vaults` and exclude `is_byo_user = true` rows from the refcount (BYO rows still gate nothing physical — their content was never on the hosted node), or record pin origin on `pinned_cids` and filter on it. If D-07 stands, document the retention consequence explicitly in `docs/CAPACITY.md`/threat model.
146	
147	## Info
148	
149	### IN-01: `fileUnpins` counter increments on no-op unpins
150	
151	**File:** `apps/api/src/vault/vault.service.ts:306`
152	**Issue:** `this.metricsService.fileUnpins.inc()` runs unconditionally — unknown-CID and cross-user no-ops count as "file unpins," inflating the metric relative to actual quota decrements.
153	**Fix:** Track whether the row was deleted (similar to `outboxRowInserted`) and increment only on that path.
154	
155	### IN-02: `UnpinDto.cid` lacks the CID format validation that `RegisterCidDto` has
156	
157	**File:** `apps/api/src/ipfs/dto/unpin.dto.ts:9-11` (adjacent to diff)
158	**Issue:** Only `@IsString()`/`@IsNotEmpty()` — no length cap or CID regex. Traced consequences are benign (parameterized SQL; `unpinFile` is only reachable for CIDs equal to stored, validated values; the warn log only fires on an equality match with a stored CID), but an arbitrary multi-megabyte string still flows into `hashtext()` and two indexed lookups per request.
159	**Fix:** Apply the same `@Matches(/^(Qm[1-9A-HJ-NP-Za-km-z]{44,}|b[a-z2-7]{58,})$/)` used in `RegisterCidDto`, plus a `@MaxLength(255)` to match the column.
160	
161	### IN-03: `recordUnpin` is now dead code
162	
163	**File:** `apps/api/src/vault/vault.service.ts:313-318`
164	**Issue:** After this phase, `recordUnpin` has zero non-test callers (confirmed by grep across `apps/api` and `apps/web`); the backfill header even notes it "had zero callers." Leaving an unguarded, non-refcounted unpin-recording method on the service invites future misuse that bypasses the D-01..D-05 properties.
165	**Fix:** Delete `recordUnpin` and its tests, or mark it `@deprecated` pointing to `guardedUnpin`.
166	
167	### IN-04: `LocalProvider` factory duplicated in three modules
168	
169	**File:** `apps/api/src/vault/vault.module.ts:23-34`, `apps/api/src/ipfs/pending-unpin/pending-unpin.module.ts:19-32`, `apps/api/src/ipfs/ipfs.module.ts:14-27`
170	**Issue:** Three identical `IPFS_PROVIDER` factories now exist to dodge the IpfsModule↔VaultModule cycle. They are consistent today, but a future provider swap in `IpfsModule.forRootAsync` would silently leave `guardedUnpin` and the drain worker talking to local Kubo.
171	**Fix:** Extract a small `IpfsProviderCoreModule` (no VaultModule dependency) that owns the factory and is imported by all three.
172	
173	### IN-05: Drift report's DB set includes BYO advisory CIDs, which can mask hosted orphans
174	
175	**File:** `apps/api/src/ipfs/pending-unpin/pending-unpin.processor.ts:81-89`
176	**Issue:** `dbCids` is built from all `pinned_cids` rows including BYO advisory rows, whose CIDs are by definition not pinned on the hosted node. A hosted Kubo pin whose only DB representation is a BYO advisory row is exactly the WR-07 retention case — and the drift report classifies it as "accounted," so it never surfaces.
177	**Fix:** Build the drift DB set from non-BYO rows (plus `pending_unpins`), consistent with whatever resolution WR-07 gets.
178	
179	### IN-06: `outboxRowInserted` is set even when `orIgnore` deduped the insert
180	
181	**File:** `apps/api/src/vault/vault.service.ts:284-291`
182	**Issue:** The flag is set unconditionally after `execute()`, so it really means "refcount hit zero," not "this request inserted the row." Behavior is harmless (the inline unpin is idempotent and concurrent deduped callers just both attempt it), but the name misleads future readers about the orIgnore semantics.
183	**Fix:** Rename to `shouldAttemptPhysicalUnpin`, or inspect `execute()`'s returned identifiers to set it accurately.
184
