28	
29	# Phase 43: Code Review Report
30	
31	**Reviewed:** 2026-06-12T19:13:45Z
32	**Depth:** standard
33	**Files Reviewed:** 14
34	**Status:** issues_found
35	
36	## Summary
37	
38	The journal primitive itself (`WriteQueue` in `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs`) is solid: fsync-before-return, 0600 perms, vault scoping, skip-on-malformed. The fsync-before-ack ordering in the happy path of both fuser and WinFsp callbacks is correct. However, the replay half of the feature is fundamentally broken, and several advertised behaviors are dead code:
39	
40	1. Replay never publishes the parent IPNS record (no key in journal), yet returns `Ok`, removes the journal entry, and unpins the CID the parent IPNS record still points to. The original UAT orphan bug is NOT fixed by replay, and replay actively risks making existing folder metadata unfetchable.
41	2. Replay misinterprets the journaled ECIES-wrapped IPNS keys as raw 32-byte Ed25519 keys, so `UploadFile` replay with a key can never succeed, and `MkdirPublish` replay writes a TEE-wrapped key where a user-wrapped key belongs.
42	3. `record_failure`, parking, and `SyncStatus::WriteParked` have zero production callers — the retry/park/notify pipeline described in the phase goal does not exist end-to-end.
43	4. The WinFsp mirror does not compile (references to nonexistent types) and Windows never calls `replay_for_vault` at all.
44	5. The fuser release error path acks `reply.ok()` after a journal failure — the exact silent-loss the fsync-before-ack invariant exists to prevent.
45	
46	## Critical Issues
47	
48	### CR-01: Replay removes journal entries without any remote commit and unpins the live parent metadata CID
49	
50	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1080-1101` (callers: `lib.rs:926-933`, `lib.rs:964-973`)
51	**Issue:** `fetch_merge_publish_parent` uploads the merged metadata to IPFS but never publishes an IPNS record (the parent IPNS private key is not journaled — acknowledged in the inline comment). It then:
52	
53	1. Returns `Ok(())`, so `replay_for_vault` calls `journal.remove(&entry.id)` — entry deleted with no confirmed remote commit. The journaled child is still absent from the parent folder listing. This is the exact orphan bug (UAT D-03) the phase claims to close; after one replay pass the journal is empty and the data is permanently orphaned.
54	2. Calls `unpin_content(api, &resolve.cid)` on the CID that the parent IPNS record STILL points to (line 1099). If GC collects it, the parent folder's current metadata becomes unfetchable — the whole subtree disappears. This is new data-loss risk introduced by this phase.
55	3. `coordinator.record_publish(parent_ipns_name, seq)` records a publish that never happened.
56	
57	**Fix:** Journal must carry enough material to complete the parent publish (e.g., the user-ECIES-wrapped parent IPNS private key, unwrappable with `private_key` at replay time — same zero-knowledge posture as `FolderEntry.ipns_private_key_encrypted`). Until the publish succeeds (`PublishResult::Success`), `fetch_merge_publish_parent` must return `Err` so the caller keeps the entry. Delete the `unpin_content(&resolve.cid)` call entirely — never unpin a CID that an IPNS record still references.
58	
59	### CR-02: UploadFile replay treats the ECIES-wrapped file IPNS key as a raw Ed25519 key — replay can never succeed
60	
61	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1195-1227` (journal write side: `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:819-832`, `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:882-895`)
62	**Issue:** The release path journals `file_ipns_key_hex` as the ECIES-wrapped key (`wrap_key(k, &fs.public_key)` → ~117+ bytes). `replay_upload_entry` hex-decodes it and does:
63	
64	```rust
65	let ipns_key_arr: [u8; 32] = file_ipns_key.as_slice().try_into()
66	    .map_err(|_| "Invalid file IPNS key length for replay".to_string())?;
67	```
68	
69	A wrapped key is never 32 bytes, so this always errors, the whole `replay_upload_entry` returns `Err`, the entry stays Pending, and is retried (and fails identically) on every subsequent mount, forever. Net effect: no `UploadFile` entry that carries a key is ever replayed or drained. Additionally, step 4 (line 1275-1282) re-wraps the already-wrapped bytes (`hex::decode(k)` then `wrap_key(&bytes, public_key)`), producing a double-wrapped `ipns_private_key_encrypted` that no client can ever decrypt — which would silently corrupt the file pointer if step 3 didn't fail first.
70	
71	**Fix:** In `replay_upload_entry`, unwrap the journaled key with the user's private key before use:
72	
73	```rust
74	let raw_key = cipherbox_crypto::ecies::unwrap_key(&hex::decode(k)?, private_key)?;
75	let ipns_key_arr: [u8; 32] = raw_key.as_slice().try_into()...;
76	```
77	
78	And in step 4 store the journaled wrapped hex as-is (it is already user-wrapped) instead of re-wrapping.
79	
80	### CR-03: MkdirPublish journals the TEE-wrapped IPNS key into a field replayed as the user-wrapped key
81	
82	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs:525`, `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:153`, replay side `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1135`
83	**Issue:** `child_ipns_key_hex: encrypted_ipns_for_tee.clone().unwrap_or_default()` stores the IPNS private key wrapped with the **TEE public key** (or an empty string when `tee_public_key` is `None`). `replay_mkdir_entry` writes that value into `FolderEntry.ipns_private_key_encrypted`, which everywhere else in the codebase is the **user-ECIES-wrapped** key (`build_folder_metadata` at `lib.rs:619`, rmdir at `write_ops.rs:716`). After replay, the folder's IPNS private key in metadata is either TEE-wrapped (the client cannot unwrap it — folder becomes permanently unpublishable from any client) or empty. This breaks the metadata schema contract and quietly bricks write access to the replayed folder.
84	
85	**Fix:** Journal the user-wrapped key (`wrap_key(&ipns_private_key, &fs.public_key)`) in `child_ipns_key_hex` — it is already computed for the parent metadata path. If the TEE-wrapped key is also needed for replaying the child's initial publish, store both as distinct fields with accurate names (`child_ipns_key_user_wrapped_hex`, `child_ipns_key_tee_wrapped_hex`).
86	
87	### CR-04: fuser release acks `reply.ok()` when journal fsync (or any prepare step) fails — silent data loss
88	
89	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:956-964`
90	**Issue:** When the prepare closure errors — including `fs.journal.put(&journal_entry)?` failing (disk full, journal dir deleted, permission error) and encryption failures — the `Err` arm logs, calls `handle.cleanup()` (deleting the plaintext temp file), then falls through to `reply.ok()` at line 964. The OS is told the close succeeded, no journal entry exists, no upload thread was spawned, and the plaintext temp file has been deleted. The write is gone with zero trace — precisely the failure mode the fsync-before-ack invariant (D-04) exists to prevent.
91	
92	**Fix:**
93	
94	```rust
95	Err(e) => {
96	    log::error!("File upload preparation failed for ino {}: {}", ino, e);
97	    handle.cleanup();
98	    reply.error(libc::EIO);
99	    return;
100	}
101	```
102	
103	(With care to roll back the inode mutations made earlier in the closure — `inode.kind` reset and `pending_content` insert happen before `journal.put`, so a late failure currently also leaves local state claiming the write succeeded.)
104	
105	### CR-05: Windows WinFsp upload path references nonexistent types — `winfsp` feature cannot compile
106	
107	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:748-751`
108	**Issue:** The `UploadSpawnParams` struct added in this phase declares:
109	
110	```rust
111	api: std::sync::Arc<cipherbox_api_client::ApiConfig>,
112	rt: std::sync::Arc<tokio::runtime::Runtime>,
113	coordinator: std::sync::Arc<crate::publish_coordinator::PublishCoordinator>,
114	```
115	
116	- `cipherbox_api_client::ApiConfig` does not exist (the client type is `ApiClient`, `crates/api-client/src/client.rs:17`); `fs.api.clone()` is `Arc<ApiClient>`.
117	- `CipherBoxFS.rt` is `tokio::runtime::Handle`, not `Arc<Runtime>`.
118	- There is no `publish_coordinator` module in the fuse crate; `PublishCoordinator` lives at crate root.
119	
120	This code is gated behind `#[cfg(feature = "winfsp")]`, which is not part of `default` features, so the macOS/Linux build being "clean" proves nothing about it. The Windows mirror of the durability fix is unverified, non-compiling code.
121	
122	**Fix:** Match the fuser struct (`Arc<ApiClient>`, `tokio::runtime::Handle`, `Arc<crate::PublishCoordinator>`) and add a CI check that compiles `--features winfsp` (e.g., `cargo check --no-default-features --features winfsp` on a Windows runner or with a stubbed winfsp dep).
123	
124	### CR-06: Windows mount never calls `replay_for_vault` — journal is write-only on Windows
125	
126	**File:** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/windows/mod.rs:66-72, 332-365`
127	**Issue:** The macOS mount path calls `cipherbox_fuse::replay_for_vault(...)` before mounting (`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs:233-242`). The Windows mount constructs the same journal and passes it into `CipherBoxFS`, but never replays. On Windows, journaled entries accumulate forever and crashed writes are never recovered — the headline durability guarantee of this phase ("replay on mount") does not exist on Windows. (The Windows coordinator is also not seeded with initial sequences, unlike macOS.)
128	
129	**Fix:** Call `replay_for_vault` after the pre-populate block and before constructing `CipherBoxFS`, mirroring `fuse/mod.rs:233-242`.
130	
131	### CR-07: Retry/park/notify pipeline is dead code — `record_failure` and `SyncStatus::WriteParked` have zero production callers
132	
133	**File:** `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:245-265`, `crates/sdk/src/state.rs:24-29`, `crates/sdk/src/sync.rs:97-136`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sync/mod.rs:39-51`
134	**Issue:** Grep confirms `record_failure` is called only from unit tests, and `SyncStatus::WriteParked` is constructed only in tests. The sync daemon emits only `Idle`/`Syncing`/`Error`. Consequences:
135	
136	- `retries` never increments; no entry ever transitions to `Failed`; `max_retries` (injected as 5) is inert.
137	- The tray `WriteParked` state and `send_write_parked_notification` are unreachable.
138	- A background upload failure during a live session is only an error log (`read_ops.rs:948-952`); the entry is never retried until the next mount, and the user is never informed. The phase's stated deliverable — "max-retry exhaustion parks the entry as Failed and surfaces `SyncStatus::WriteParked` → tray + OS notification" — is not implemented anywhere.
139	
140	Additionally, `SyncDaemon` constructs `WriteQueue::default()` (`sync.rs:64`) pointing at `temp_dir/cipherbox-journal-default`, a directory that is never created and is unrelated to the real `cb-journal` dir — the daemon cannot even see the real journal.
141	
142	**Fix:** Inject the real journal (dir) into `SyncDaemon`; in `sync_cycle`, load pending/failed counts via `load_all_for_vault` and emit `WriteParked` accordingly; have the background-upload failure paths (`read_ops.rs:948`, windows `write_ops.rs:1002`) call `record_failure` on the persisted entry.
143	
144	### CR-08: Journal entry removed after ciphertext upload but before the parent folder pointer is committed
145	
146	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:941-943` (Windows: `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:996-997`)
147	**Issue:** The release upload thread removes the journal entry once `upload_content` (and a best-effort `publish_file_metadata`) finish. But the parent folder pointer publish — the thing that makes the file visible — happens later via the debounced publisher (`queue_publish` → `flush_publish_queue`, 1.5s+ debounce), is not journaled, and uses live inode state. A crash or quit in that window leaves a pinned, IPNS-published file that no folder metadata references, with an empty journal: unrecoverable orphan. Also note the entry is removed even when `publish_file_metadata` **failed** (only `log::warn!` at `read_ops.rs:933`), so "removal only after confirmed remote commit" is violated on two counts.
148	
149	**Fix:** Remove the entry only after the parent folder publish for this file is confirmed (e.g., signal back through `UploadComplete`/the debounced publisher, removing the entry when the parent publish containing this `file_meta_ipns_name` succeeds), and only when `publish_file_metadata` succeeded.
150	
151	## Warnings
152	
153	### WR-01: Replay order is nondeterministic — `created_at_ms` is never used
154	
155	**File:** `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:272-285`, `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:171-222`
156	**Issue:** `load_all_for_vault` returns entries in `read_dir` order, which is filesystem-arbitrary (not insertion order). `ordered_for_replay` only partitions Mkdir-before-Upload and "preserves relative order" of an already-arbitrary input. Nested offline mkdirs (`a/` then `a/b/`) can replay child-before-parent; two writes to the same file can replay newest-then-oldest, resurrecting stale content as the final state.
157	**Fix:** Sort each group by `created_at_ms` (already serialized in every op) before replay.
158	
159	### WR-02: `resolve_folder_key` only searches the root's direct children — replay fails for anything nested deeper than one level
160	
161	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1006-1018`
162	**Issue:** Only `root_meta.children` is scanned for the parent folder's wrapped key. Any journaled write whose parent is two or more levels deep returns "folder IPNS not found in root metadata" on every mount, forever (no retry cap per CR-07). The mounted FS supports arbitrary nesting, so this is a common case, not an edge.
163	**Fix:** Recursive/BFS traversal of folder metadata (resolving each subfolder's key as you descend), or journal the parent's wrapped folder key alongside the entry.
164	
165	### WR-03: Journal file permission race and missing parent-directory fsync
166	
167	**File:** `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:126-150`
168	**Issue:** (a) The file is created with default (umask-derived) permissions and only chmod'd to 0600 after the ciphertext and wrapped keys are fully written and fsynced — a window where other local users may read it on permissive umasks. (b) `sync_all` on the file does not persist the new directory entry; on ext4/APFS a crash immediately after `put()` can lose the file itself, so the "durable before ack" guarantee is weaker than claimed.
169	**Fix:** (a) `OpenOptions::new().mode(0o600)` (via `std::os::unix::fs::OpenOptionsExt`) at create time. (b) Open and `sync_all()` the journal directory after creating the file.
170	
171	### WR-04: mkdir leaves a ghost inode in the local table when a late prepare step fails
172	
173	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs:482-534` (Windows: `platform/windows/write_ops.rs:116-162`)
174	**Issue:** The new folder inode is inserted into `fs.inodes` and the parent's children before `build_folder_metadata` and the newly added `fs.journal.put(...)?`. If either fails, the kernel gets EIO but the local table keeps the directory; subsequent readdir shows a folder the OS believes was never created, and the debounced publisher can publish the phantom entry to remote metadata. `journal.put` adds a new failure point after the insertion.
175	**Fix:** On the `Err` path, remove the inode and the parent-children entry (mirroring `handle_create`'s rollback at `write_ops.rs:230-241`), or perform journal.put before inserting the inode.
176	
177	### WR-05: mkdir-conflict retry path never removes the journal entry after the re-armed publish succeeds
178	
179	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs:631-641`, `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:686-692`
180	**Issue:** On parent-publish conflict the journal entry is deliberately retained and `FsEvent::MkdirConflict` re-arms the debounced publisher — but the debounced path (`flush_publish_queue` → `spawn_metadata_publish`) has no knowledge of the journal entry and never removes it on success. The entry stays Pending for the rest of the session and is replayed on the next mount; the only cleanup is replay's `already_present` idempotency short-circuit, which never runs on Windows (CR-06) and sits inside the broken replay path (CR-01).
181	**Fix:** Thread the journal entry id through the conflict event so the successful debounced publish removes it, or have replay's idempotency check be the documented and tested cleanup mechanism once CR-01/CR-06 are fixed.
182	
183	### WR-06: Unbounded journal growth and full-ciphertext-in-JSON design
184	
185	**File:** `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:120-153`, `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:808-844`
186	**Issue:** Each `UploadFile` entry embeds the entire file ciphertext as base64 inside a JSON document: a 2 GB file becomes a ~2.7 GB allocation in `serde_json::to_vec`, then a multi-GB write + F_FULLFSYNC executed on the single FUSE callback thread (macOS) or while holding the global WinFsp mutex (Windows) — blocking the whole filesystem for the duration, and capable of aborting on memory. There is no size cap, no GC of parked `Failed` entries, and entries from other vaults persist forever after account switch (the `cb-journal` dir is shared and only ever filtered, never pruned).
187	**Fix:** Store ciphertext in a sidecar file (`<id>.bin`) streamed to disk with the JSON holding only its path/hash; cap journaled payload size or fall back to a temp-ciphertext-file reference; add GC for parked entries (age/size budget) and an explicit purge on logout/account deletion.
188	
189	### WR-07: `replay_for_vault` runs inline in mount with no network timeouts
190	
191	**File:** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs:233-242`, `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:870-977`
192	**Issue:** Replay awaits raw `resolve_ipns`/`fetch_content`/`upload_content` calls per entry (several round trips each) with none of the `NETWORK_TIMEOUT` discipline used elsewhere in the crate (`block_with_timeout`, `lib.rs:54-69`). A hung connection during replay stalls `mount_filesystem` indefinitely; many entries on a slow link delay mount by minutes.
193	**Fix:** Wrap each entry's replay in `tokio::time::timeout(NETWORK_TIMEOUT * k, ...)`, and/or run replay concurrently with (not before) mount.
194	
195	### WR-08: Empty `file_meta_ipns_name` is journaled and replayed into parent metadata
196	
197	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:803-805` (Windows: `write_ops.rs:866-868`), replay `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1271-1294`
198	**Issue:** `file_meta_ipns_name.clone().unwrap_or_default()` journals an empty string when the inode lacks a file IPNS name. Replay then merges a `FilePointer` with `file_meta_ipns_name: ""` into the remote parent metadata — an invalid child that downstream consumers skip with errors (`build_folder_metadata` logs "has no fileMetaIpnsName") and that the `already_present` check matches against any other empty-keyed entry.
199	**Fix:** Refuse to journal (or refuse to replay) `UploadFile` entries with an empty `file_meta_ipns_name`; treat it as a prepare error.
200	
201	### WR-09: `WriteQueue::Default` points at a nonexistent temp directory
202	
203	**File:** `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:288-292`, `crates/sdk/src/sync.rs:64`
204	**Issue:** `Default` resolves to `temp_dir()/cipherbox-journal-default`, which is never created (the constructor documents "directory must already exist"), so any `put` through it fails; and temp dirs are wiped on reboot, contradicting the durability purpose. Its only consumer is the dead `SyncDaemon.write_queue` field (see CR-07).
205	**Fix:** Remove the `Default` impl and the daemon's `write_queue`/`write_queue_mut` until the daemon is wired to the real journal.
206	
207	## Info
208	
209	### IN-01: Off-by-one in retry accounting
210	
211	**File:** `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:250`
212	**Issue:** Parking happens when `retries >= max_retries` after the increment path, so `max_retries = 5` yields 6 attempts. Harmless but the name lies.
213	**Fix:** Park when `entry.retries + 1 >= max_retries`, or document the semantics.
214	
215	### IN-02: One bad directory entry aborts the whole journal load
216	
217	**File:** `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:180-182`
218	**Issue:** `dir_entry.map_err(...)?` fails the entire `load_all_for_vault` on a single transient `read_dir` entry error, unlike the skip-with-warn handling of unreadable/malformed files just below.
219	**Fix:** `continue` with a `log::warn!` instead of `?`.
220	
221	### IN-03: Plaintext file and directory names persisted in journal JSON
222	
223	**File:** `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:33-34, 50-51`
224	**Issue:** `filename` / `name` are stored in cleartext on disk (0600, local-only). Content and keys are protected, but this is a new at-rest disclosure of vault item names not previously persisted anywhere locally. Worth a threat-model note; not a zero-knowledge violation toward the server.
225	**Fix:** Acceptable as-is if documented; alternatively encrypt names with the user public key like other metadata.
226	
227	### IN-04: `sanitize_error` path scrubbing misses Windows and other Unix paths
228	
229	**File:** `crates/sdk/src/sync.rs:226-245`
230	**Issue:** Only `/Users/` and `/home/` prefixes are scrubbed; `C:\Users\...`, `/var/`, `/tmp/`, `/private/` leak through into tray/notification copy.
231	**Fix:** Extend the prefix list and add a drive-letter pattern.
232	
233	### IN-05: Journal removal errors silently swallowed
234	
235	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:943`, `crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs:628`, `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:929, 968`
236	**Issue:** `let _ = journal.remove(...)` everywhere. A failed removal means the entry replays later; combined with the idempotency gaps above this can double-publish. At minimum log the error.
237	**Fix:** `if let Err(e) = journal.remove(...) { log::warn!(...) }`.
238	
239	### IN-06: `record_publish` called for a publish that never happened
240	
241	**File:** `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1097`
242	**Issue:** `fetch_merge_publish_parent` seeds the sequence cache via `record_publish(parent_ipns_name, seq)` despite publishing nothing. Currently masked by CR-01; remove when reworking that function so the cache only reflects confirmed publishes.
243	**Fix:** Delete the call; `resolve_sequence` already updated the cache with the resolved value.
244	
245	## Post-Review Resolution (2026-06-14)
246	
247	All 8 critical findings (CR-01..CR-08) were verified resolved via a code cross-check against the current implementation and a CodeRabbit re-review on 2026-06-14.
248	
249	| Finding | Status | Resolution (commits) |
250	| ------- | ------ | -------------------- |
251	| CR-01 | FIXED | Replay now journals the user-ECIES-wrapped parent IPNS key, CAS-publishes the parent IPNS record, returns `Err` until `PublishResult::Success` (entry retained on failure), and unpins only in the post-Success arm against the stale pre-merge CID (4bc1a0278, 0b8545bad, 7633cf795). |
252	| CR-02 | FIXED | `replay_upload_entry` now unwraps the journaled key with the user private key before the `[u8;32]` conversion, and step 4 stores the journaled wrapped hex as-is with no double-wrap (4bc1a0278, 0b8545bad). |
253	| CR-03 | FIXED | Both fuser and winfsp paths journal the user-ECIES-wrapped child IPNS key; the TEE-wrapped key is segregated to the live publish request, the residual TEE fallback was removed, and journal IPNS-key wraps now warn-and-empty on failure (this change). |
254	| CR-04 | FIXED | The fuser prepare-failure path returns `reply.error(EIO)` instead of `reply.ok()`, and all in-memory mutations (inode kind/attr, pending_content, queued publish) are deferred until after the journal fsync, so a failure leaves no partial state and needs no rollback; Windows Cleanup returns VOID, so the same no-mutation-on-failure deferral is its mitigation (4e8c48020, this change). |
255	| CR-05 | FIXED | `UploadSpawnParams` now uses `Arc<ApiClient>`, `tokio::runtime::Handle`, and `Arc<crate::PublishCoordinator>`; CI compiles `cargo check --features winfsp` (963468eed). |
256	| CR-06 | FIXED | Windows mount now calls `replay_for_vault` and seeds the coordinator, mirroring macOS (ad2339d7e). |
257	| CR-07 | FIXED | `SyncDaemon` is wired to the real cb-journal `WriteQueue` (Default impl removed); `sync_cycle` loads entries via `load_all_for_vault` and emits `SyncStatus::WriteParked`, background-upload failures call `record_failure`, and the tray surfaces WriteParked plus a notification (d5cae52fc, 0d4cc08c4, 7633cf795, 7a9f3ca75, 5b6455c34). |
258	| CR-08 | FIXED | Entry removal is now replay-only and parent-publish-gated; the live upload thread no longer removes entries and `publish_file_metadata` failure no longer triggers removal (4e8c48020, 293de3f4c). |

Document a runbook (always UPDATE the CVM, never delete+recreate) and a re-enrollment path. — `35-05-SUMMARY` / `35-VERIFICATION` truth 21\n- [ ] **[P29·med]** Staging/prod IPFS API (Kubo `:5001`) is bound to all interfaces in `docker-compose.yml` with an unresolved TODO for env-specific hardening (127.0.0.1 binding / reverse proxy + API auth). — `docker/docker-compose.yml:37`. Overlaps 📋 BACKLOG \"Kubo API access control (reverse proxy or ACL)\".\n- [ ] **[P19.2·info]** Deployment coupling: SDK concurrent pins regress performance (+138% p95 at 50 clients) on `flatfs` — they are **synergistic with the `pebbleds` datastore and must be deployed together**. Both shipped, but document the coupling so a datastore rollback doesn't silently regress uploads. — `19.2-04-SUMMARY`\n\n## 7. Already tracked in `.planning/BACKLOG.md` (📋 not duplicated)\n\nThese v1.1 deferrals are already in the backlog inventory and remain correctly tracked there — see [`../BACKLOG.md`](../BACKLOG.md):\n\n- **Sharing (P14/P27):** metadata-embedded sharing, attribution/`lastModifiedBy`, transitive re-sharing, share notifications, immediate key rotation on revoke, faster shared-folder sync, CRDT IPNS inbox.\n- **Desktop (P25/P14/P17):** platform code signing (Apple notarization), beta/canary channels, delta updates, retroactive TEE enrollment, desktop sharing/bin/search UI, `.Trash` integration.\n- **Upload pipeline (P37):** adaptive concurrency, FUSE write-coalescing, accumulated retry batching, AbortSignal cancellation, lazy file reading in the pool.\n- **Observability (P28/P30):** `no-console` ESLint enforcement, Web Worker logging bridge, \"Report a problem\" button.\n- **Infra/data (P29/P12.6/P17):** periodic unenroll reconciliation job, TEE enrollment drift reconciliation, Kubo API ACL.\n- **Code quality:** `uint8ToBase64` dedup (Tier 3.3 — verified **3 copies** still: `sdk-core/file`, `sdk-core/folder`, `web/file-metadata.service`), plus Tier-3 cleanups 3.1–3.14.\n- **Security review residue (P14/PR #448):** M1 (`itemName` plaintext — note: now mitigated for shares via Phase 48 ECIES `itemNameEncrypted`), M5 (`reWrapForRecipients` residual caller), S1/S2/S3 (IPNS signed-record validation/enforcement/key-zeroization).\n\n## 8. Deferred to v1.2+ (📋 out of scope by design)\n\nPer `REQUIREMENTS.md` and `PROJECT.md`: CRDT-based IPNS inbox for share discovery (IPNS-05), advisory `folder_ipns` CID cache (IPNS-06), device-registry off DB (DB-01), `pinned_cids` elimination (DB-02), client-direct IPFS upload bypassing relay (BYO-08). Milestone 4 (v2.0): Encrypted Productivity Suite, mobile apps, real-time collaboration.\n\n---\n\n## Summary\n\n| Bucket | Count | Tracking |\n| ------ | ----- | -------- |\n| Phase 42 unpin-integrity review (open) | 12 | 🆕 1 new todo |\n| Phase 43 FUSE-journal review (open) | 5 | 🆕 1 new todo |\n| Web observability wiring | 3 | 🆕 1 new todo |\n| IPNS unenroll completeness | 1 | 🆕 1 new todo |\n| GSD verification gaps | 3 | 🆕 1 new todo |\n| Operational / known limitations | 3 | 🆕 documented here |\n| Already in BACKLOG | ~30 | 📋 referenced |\n| Deferred to v1.2+ | — | 📋 referenced |\n\n**Net-new tracked this pass:** 5 pending-todo files in `.planning/todos/pending/` (dated 2026-06-18), covering 24 verified-open items across phases 42, 43, 28/30, 29, and the 18/31/32 process gaps.   "content": "---\ncreated: 2026-06-18T00:00:00.000Z\ntitle: Phase 42 unpin-integrity code-review findings (WR/IN) are unresolved in current code\narea: bug\nseverity: high\nsource: .planning/phases/42-api-unpin-integrity/42-REVIEW.md (no resolution section); re-verified against live code 2026-06-18\nfiles:\n  - apps/api/src/vault/vault.service.ts\n  - apps/api/src/ipfs/ipfs.controller.ts\n  - apps/api/src/ipfs/pending-unpin/pending-unpin.processor.ts\n  - apps/api/src/ipfs/dto/unpin.dto.ts\n  - scripts/backfill-pinned-cids.ts\n---\n\n## Problem\n\n`42-REVIEW.md` raised 7 warnings + 6 info findings on the guarded-unpin / pending-unpins\nimplementation and has **no resolution section**. Each was re-checked against current code on\n2026-06-18 and confirmed still present. Two are correctness risks:\n\n- **WR-01 (high)** — `vault.service.ts:262` `SELECT abs(hashtext($1))::bigint`. For the CID whose\n  `hashtext == INT_MIN (-2147483648)`, `abs(int4)` raises `ERROR: integer out of range` —\n  deterministically, every unpin of that CID 500s, and its quota row is permanently stuck. The\n  file becomes **permanently undeletable via the API**. (`pg_advisory_xact_lock` accepts signed\n  bigint, so the `abs()` is both unnecessary and the only failure mode.)\n- **WR-03 (high)** — `pending-unpin.processor.ts:53` `drainPendingUnpins` unpins every outbox CID\n  unconditionally. If a CID is re-pinned/re-recorded (re-upload of identical ciphertext, or a\n  pin-migration flow) while still in `pending_unpins`, the next drain pass removes a **live pin**\n  → content eligible for Kubo GC → **data loss**. The D-13 \"sub-second window\" rationale does not\n  cover the outbox path (window ≥ 5 min, unbounded while Kubo is down).\n\nMedium/low findings: WR-02 (upload-compensation `guardedUnpin` no-op leaks the Kubo pin and can\nfire the cross-user security alert on internal failures), WR-05 (backfill TOCTOU deletes in-flight\nupload rows as phantoms), WR-07 (BYO advisory rows block physical unpin of hosted content\nindefinitely — non-owner-controllable retention path), WR-06 (backfill hardcodes\n`false::boolean AS \"isByoUser\"`, defeating the defensive re-assert), IN-01 (`fileUnpins.inc()` on\nno-ops), IN-02 (`UnpinDto.cid` missing CID regex/MaxLength), IN-03 (`recordUnpin` dead code),\nIN-04 (`IPFS_PROVIDER` factory duplicated ×3), IN-05 (drift `dbCids` includes BYO), IN-06\n(`outboxRowInserted` misnamed). WR-04 (Counter vs Gauge) was reviewed and judged acceptable.\n\n## Fix\n\n- **WR-01:** drop `abs()` (`SELECT hashtext($1)::bigint`) or cast first (`abs(hashtext($1)::bigint)`).\n- **WR-03:** in the drain loop, `count` `pinned_cids` for the CID before `unpinFile`; if `> 0`, delete\n  the stale outbox row and `continue`.\n- **WR-02/05/06/07 + IN-\\*:** apply the per-finding fixes in `42-REVIEW.md` (each has a concrete patch).\n\n## Acceptance\n\nWR-01 and WR-03 fixed with regression tests (INT_MIN CID undeletability; re-pin-during-pending\ndrain must not unpin). Remaining WR/IN items resolved or explicitly accepted with a comment.\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.planning/todos/pending/2026-06-18-fuse-journal-growth-and-replay-timeout.md",
  "content": "---\ncreated: 2026-06-18T00:00:00.000Z\ntitle: FUSE write-journal unbounded growth + ciphertext-in-JSON, and replay has no network timeout\narea: bug\nseverity: high\nsource: .planning/phases/43-fuse-write-durability/43-REVIEW.md warnings (criticals fixed 2026-06-14; warnings re-verified against live code 2026-06-18, post phases 45/46)\nfiles:\n  - crates/sdk/src/queue.rs\n  - crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs\n  - crates/fuse/src/lib.rs\n  - crates/sdk/src/sync.rs\n  - apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs\n---\n\n## Problem\n\nAll 8 critical findings (CR-01..CR-08) in `43-REVIEW.md` were verified FIXED on 2026-06-14, and\nphases 45/46 resolved most warnings (replay ordering, BFS folder-key resolution, atomic 0600 +\nparent-dir fsync, mkdir rollback, conflict entry-id threading, empty-name guard, `Default` removed).\nRe-verified 2026-06-18 — these remain **open**:\n\n- **WR-06 (high)** — Each `UploadFile` journal entry embeds the **entire file ciphertext as base64\n  inside the JSON document**. A 2 GB file → ~2.7 GB allocation in `serde_json::to_vec`, then a\n  multi-GB write + `F_FULLFSYNC` executed **on the single FUSE callback thread** (macOS) / while\n  holding the global WinFsp mutex (Windows) — blocking the whole filesystem for the duration and\n  capable of OOM. There is no size cap, no GC of parked `Failed` entries, and entries from other\n  vaults persist forever after account switch (the journal dir is shared, only ever filtered).\n  (`crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:36`)\n- **WR-07 (med)** — `replay_for_vault` awaits raw `resolve_ipns`/`fetch_content`/`upload_content`\n  per entry with none of the `NETWORK_TIMEOUT` discipline used elsewhere. A hung connection stalls\n  `mount_filesystem` indefinitely; many entries on a slow link delay mount by minutes.\n  (`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs:278`)\n- **IN-03 (low)** — plaintext `filename`/`name` persisted in journal JSON (new local at-rest\n  disclosure of vault item names; 0600, local-only). (`crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:62`)\n- **IN-04 (low)** — `sanitize_error` only scrubs `/Users/` and `/home/`; `C:\\Users\\…`, `/var`,\n  `/tmp`, `/private` leak into tray/notification copy. (`crates/sdk/src/sync.rs:271`)\n- **IN-05 (low)** — `let _ = journal.remove(...)` swallows removal errors → silent later replay /\n  double-publish risk. (`crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:1494`, `:1558`; `write_ops.rs:679`)\n\n## Fix\n\n- **WR-06:** store ciphertext in a sidecar `<id>.bin` streamed to disk; JSON holds only path/hash.\n  Cap journaled payload size. Add GC for parked entries (age/size budget) and an explicit purge on\n  logout / account deletion.\n- **WR-07:** wrap each entry's replay in `tokio::time::timeout(NETWORK_TIMEOUT * k, ...)` and/or run\n  replay concurrently with (not before) mount.\n- **IN-03/04/05:** document/encrypt journaled names; extend the path-scrub prefix list + drive-letter\n  pattern; `log::warn!` on removal errors.\n\n## Acceptance\n\nLarge-file write no longer blocks the FS thread (sidecar ciphertext); journal has a bounded\ngrowth/GC story and a logout purge; replay cannot hang the mount.\n"
}

  "content": "---\ncreated: 2026-06-18T00:00:00.000Z\ntitle: Web logger redaction interceptor missing and Faro transport never wired\narea: observability\nseverity: medium\nsource: Phase 28 (CONTEXT D-03/D-04) vs Phase 30 (deferred registerFaroTransport); verified against live code 2026-06-18\nfiles:\n  - apps/web/src/lib/logger.ts\n  - apps/web/src/lib/faro.ts\n  - apps/web/src/main.tsx\n---\n\n## Problem\n\nPhase 28 specified (D-03) a `redact()` interceptor that strips sensitive fields\n(`privateKey`/`rootFolderKey`/`folderKey`/`fileKey`/`accessToken`) from log context, and (D-04) a\n`transports[]` hook array. Phase 30 was to call `registerFaroTransport(logger.transports)` so\nwarn/error logs reach Grafana Faro. Verified 2026-06-18:\n\n- `apps/web/src/lib/logger.ts` does **level filtering only** — no `redact()` interceptor and no\n  `transports[]` hook array exist. Sensitive fields passed in log context are not stripped.\n- `registerFaroTransport` **is defined** in `apps/web/src/lib/faro.ts:177` but is **never called**\n  (`initFaro()` in `main.tsx` does not call it), so warn/error logs are **not forwarded to Faro** —\n  client-side error/perf telemetry is silently incomplete.\n\nThis is the deferral noted in `30-04-SUMMARY` (\"registerFaroTransport deferred because Phase 28\nlogger shipped later\") that was never closed.\n\n## Fix\n\n1. Add a `transports: LogTransport[]` array to `logger.ts` and invoke each on warn/error.\n2. Add a `redact(context)` interceptor applied before any transport/console emit (reuse the Faro\n   `beforeSend` scrub field list for parity).\n3. Call `registerFaroTransport(logger.transports)` from Faro init (after `initFaro()`), guarded so\n   it is a no-op when Faro is disabled (`VITE_FARO_URL` absent).\n\n## Acceptance\n\nA warn/error log with a sensitive field is redacted in both console and Faro, and a forced error is\nvisible in Faro from a build with `VITE_FARO_URL` set.\n"
}

  "content": "---\ncreated: 2026-06-18T00:00:00.000Z\ntitle: collectSubtreeIpnsNames skips unloaded subtrees, leaving nested IPNS records un-unenrolled\narea: bug\nseverity: medium\nsource: Phase 29 (29-02-SUMMARY key-decisions); verified against live code 2026-06-18\nfiles:\n  - packages/sdk/src/client.ts\n---\n\n## Problem\n\nPhase 29 wired TEE/IPNS unenrollment into all four deletion paths via\n`collectSubtreeIpnsNames`, but the collector only walks **already-loaded** folder state\n(`folderTree.get()` and an early return when a folder is not loaded —\n`packages/sdk/src/client.ts:232`). Deleting a folder whose subtree was never expanded in the\nsession returns only the top folder's IPNS name, so **nested file/folder IPNS records below an\nunloaded subtree are never unenrolled**. Those records keep being republished by the TEE (wasting\ncompute) and linger until natural expiry.\n\n## Fix\n\nResolve and traverse the persisted folder metadata for the subtree being deleted (fetch+decrypt\nchild folder metadata on demand) rather than relying on the in-memory `folderTree`, so the full set\nof descendant IPNS names is collected regardless of load state. Alternatively, pair with the\n\"periodic unenroll reconciliation job\" already in BACKLOG as a backstop.\n\n## Acceptance\n\nDeleting a folder with an unloaded subtree unenrolls every descendant file/folder IPNS name (assert\nthe unenroll batch count matches the full subtree, not just loaded nodes).\n"
}

Let me capture one reusable learning from this session (the workflow structured-output pitfall) to memory, then I'll summarize.

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_01MtivQ1JEifN1NyM5dsGPHz
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/myankelev/.claude/projects/-Users-myankelev-Code-random-cipher-box/memory/feedback-workflow-large-nested-schema-synth-loop.md",
  "content": "---\nname: feedback-workflow-large-nested-schema-synth-loop\ndescription: Workflow synthesize agents loop on schema validation when asked to emit a large deeply-nested StructuredOutput in one call\nmetadata:\n  type: feedback\n---\n\nA Workflow \"synthesize/dedup\" agent given a big input (e.g. 292 swept items) and a deeply-nested\noutput schema (groups → items → 8 required fields) gets stuck in a `StructuredOutput` validation\nretry loop (\"must have required property 'summary'/'groups'\") — it can't emit the whole nested\nobject in one valid tool call, burning ~13 min with no result.\n\n**Why:** large nested structured emits exceed what the model reliably produces in a single\nvalidated call; each failure retries the entire emit.\n\n**How to apply:** for fan-out→synthesize pipelines, have sweep agents return structured items\n(small schema is fine), but do the final dedup/organize **in the orchestrator** (main loop), not a\nsingle mega-schema agent — extract the completed sweep agents' results from their\n`agent-*.jsonl` transcripts (parse the last `tool_use` whose input has the expected key) if the\nsynth agent is still stuck, then `TaskStop` the workflow. If an agent must synthesize, ask for\n**flat markdown** output (no schema) or chunk the emit. Relevant to [[project-resume-background-worktree-agents]].\n"
}
```

1	# Roadmap: CipherBox v1.1 IPFS Infrastructure
2	
3	## Overview
4	
5	CipherBox v1.1 transforms the platform from "IPFS as a storage backend with database fallbacks" to "IPFS-native with the database serving only auth." The milestone establishes performance baselines before making changes, replaces the unreliable delegated-ipfs.dev dependency with self-hosted IPNS resolution, migrates rootFolderKey to an IPFS vault blob (achieving true zero-knowledge server), adds BYO-IPFS node support for data sovereignty, and completes performance baselines with client-side instrumentation and load testing after all features are stable.
6	
7	## Milestones
8	
9	- **v0.1 Staging MVP** - Phases 1-10 (shipped 2026-02-11)
10	- **v1.0 Production** - Phases 11-17.1 (shipped 2026-03-05)
11	- **v1.1 IPFS Infrastructure** - Phases 18-22 (in progress)
12	
13	## Phases
14	
15	**Phase Numbering:**
16	
17	- Integer phases (1, 2, 3): Planned milestone work
18	- Decimal phases (2.1, 2.2): Urgent insertions (marked with INSERTED)
19	
20	Decimal phases appear between their surrounding integers in numeric order.
21	
22	- [x] **Phase 18: Performance Instrumentation** - Server-side Prometheus histograms and Kubo metrics scraping to establish baselines before any architectural changes (completed 2026-03-07)
23	- [x] **Phase 19: IPNS Resolution Improvement** - Replace delegated-ipfs.dev with self-hosted Someguy sidecar for reliable IPNS routing, add latency histograms for resolve/publish operations (completed 2026-03-07)
24	- [x] **Phase 19.2: IPFS Upload Performance Optimization** - Optimize Kubo pinning path (concurrent pins, worker tuning, pin batching) to reduce upload latency (INSERTED) (completed 2026-03-23)
25	- [x] **Phase 20: Vault Migration** - Move rootFolderKey to IPFS vault blob v2 format, making the server store zero crypto material (gap closure in progress) (completed 2026-03-24)
26	- [x] **Phase 21: BYO-IPFS Node Support** - User-configurable IPFS pinning endpoint with dual-pin strategy, Settings UI, and connection testing (gap closure in progress) (completed 2026-03-25)
27	- [x] **Phase 22: Performance Baselines Completion** - Client-side timing instrumentation, end-to-end journey timing, Vitest-based load testing, and capacity documentation (completed 2026-03-25)
28	- [x] **Phase 23: Rust SDK Extraction** - Extract five Rust crates (crypto, core, api-client, fuse, sdk) mirroring the TypeScript SDK hierarchy, replace duplicated logic in desktop FUSE code, enable unit testing at same granularity as TypeScript (completed 2026-03-24)
29	- [x] **Phase 24: Bug Fixes & Test Infrastructure** - Fix known bugs (bin IPNS 404, device registry format error) and strengthen test infrastructure (headless load tests, vault recovery E2E, load test auth refresh) (completed 2026-03-25)
30	- [x] **Phase 25: Desktop Enhancements** - Desktop auto-update mechanism and TEE file enrollment for new files (completed 2026-03-25)
31	- [x] **Phase 26: Observability & UX Tuning** - Grafana alerting thresholds from existing baselines and timeout tuning for sub-2s UX (completed 2026-03-26)
32	- [x] **Phase 28: Code Hygiene & Logging** - Structured logger wrapper, replace 124 console.\* calls, fix silenced unpin failures, clean any casts, archive legacy POC (completed 2026-03-28)
33	- [x] **Phase 29: Infrastructure Hardening** - Wire up IPNS unenrollment on deletion, test login endpoint hardening, IPFS node access control (completed 2026-03-28)
34	- [x] **Phase 30: Web App Observability** - Error tracking service, error boundaries, client-side telemetry (completed 2026-03-28)
35	- [x] **Phase 31: Structural Decomposition** - Split monolithic files (useSharedNavigation, FileBrowser, folder.service) into focused modules (completed 2026-03-28)
36	- [x] **Phase 32: FUSE Async FilePointer Resolution** - Channel-based async resolution to prevent Finder disconnects from blocking FUSE thread (completed 2026-03-28)
37	- [x] **Phase 33: Windows Async FilePointer Resolution** - Port Phase 32's channel-based async FilePointer resolution to the WinFsp backend (completed 2026-03-28)
38	- [x] **Phase 34: E2E Test Expansion & Staging Baselines** - Streaming playback, media preview, batch download, and shared teardown E2E tests; BYO-IPFS load test and Faro metrics baselines on staging (completed 2026-03-29)
39	- [x] **Phase 35: Phala Testnet TEE Migration** - Replace staging TEE simulator with real Phala testnet CVM deployment, validate hardware-backed key derivation and IPNS republishing end-to-end (completed 2026-03-29)
40	
41	## Phase Details
42	
43	### Phase 18: Performance Instrumentation
44	
45	**Goal**: Operators can observe IPFS/IPNS latency and API performance in Prometheus/Grafana before any architectural changes are made
46	**Depends on**: Nothing (first phase of milestone)
47	**Requirements**: PERF-01, PERF-02, PERF-03, PERF-04
48	**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):
49	
50	1. Prometheus exposes duration histograms for IPNS resolve, IPNS publish, IPFS pin, and IPFS cat operations with per-operation labels
51	2. API endpoint response times are captured at p50/p95/p99 for all critical routes (auth, IPNS resolve/publish, file upload/download, folder CRUD)
52	3. Kubo node health metrics (peer count, bandwidth, datastore size) are visible in Prometheus via scraped Kubo endpoint
53	4. TEE republish batch duration histogram captures per-batch timing with success/failure labels
54	
55	**Plans:** 2/2 plans complete
56	
57	Plans:
58	
59	- [x] 18-01-PLAN.md -- Add IPFS/IPNS + TEE duration histograms to MetricsService and instrument service/controller timing
60	- [x] 18-02-PLAN.md -- Add Kubo scrape to Alloy, extend Grafana dashboard, create baseline benchmark script
61	
62	### Phase 19: IPNS Resolution Improvement
63	
64	**Goal**: Users experience reliable, fast IPNS resolution without dependency on external delegated-ipfs.dev service
65	**Depends on**: Phase 18 (baselines must exist to measure improvement)
66	**Requirements**: IPNS-01, IPNS-02, IPNS-03, IPNS-04
67	**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):
68	
69	1. Self-hosted Someguy is deployed alongside Kubo and serves as the IPNS routing provider, with delegated-ipfs.dev fully removed from the resolution path
70	2. IPNS resolution completes within 2 seconds in the normal case (DB-first with async DHT verification) and degrades gracefully to DB-only when DHT is slow
71	3. The standalone recovery tool resolves IPNS records via self-hosted Someguy without depending on the CipherBox API or delegated-ipfs.dev
72	4. No user-visible errors or stale metadata when the DHT is temporarily unreachable (DB fallback serves correct data)
73	   **Plans**: 2 plans
74	
75	Plans:
76	
77	- [x] 19-01-PLAN.md -- Deploy Someguy Docker sidecar, update routing URL in deploy workflow and .env.example
78	- [x] 19-02-PLAN.md -- Add IPNS resolve/publish latency histograms and instrument IpnsService with timing
79	
80	### Phase 19.1: Extract core crypto SDK as shared package (INSERTED)
81	
82	**Goal:** Web app's crypto and file operation logic is extracted into a five-package layered SDK architecture (@cipherbox/crypto, @cipherbox/core, @cipherbox/api-client, @cipherbox/sdk-core, @cipherbox/sdk) enabling load testing, integration testing, and future CLI usage without a browser context
83	**Requirements**: SDK-01, SDK-02, SDK-03, SDK-04, SDK-05, SDK-06, SDK-07, SDK-08, SDK-09, SDK-10, SDK-11
84	**Depends on:** Phase 19
85	**Plans:** 6/6 plans complete
86	
87	Plans:
88	
89	- [ ] 19.1-01-PLAN.md -- Split crypto into crypto + core packages with transitional re-exports
90	- [ ] 19.1-02-PLAN.md -- Expand api-client package with orval generation and configurable instance
91	- [ ] 19.1-03-PLAN.md -- Create sdk-core with stateless folder/file/IPFS/IPNS operations
92	- [ ] 19.1-04-PLAN.md -- Create sdk with stateful CipherBoxClient, events, bin, and share operations
93	- [ ] 19.1-05-PLAN.md -- Rewire web app hooks and stores to use SDK
94	- [ ] 19.1-06-PLAN.md -- Remove re-exports, update imports, configure Release Please
95	
96	### Phase 19.2: IPFS Upload Performance Optimization (INSERTED)
97	
98	**Goal:** Upload operations are measurably faster by optimizing the Kubo IPFS pinning path — the dominant bottleneck consuming ~95% of upload endpoint latency (~1.73s mean per pin, 3 sequential pins per upload)
99	**Depends on:** Phase 19.1 (SDK needed for load test benchmarking)
100	**Requirements**: PERF-09
101	**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):
102	
103	1. Independent pin operations within a single upload (ciphertext + file metadata) execute concurrently rather than sequentially, reducing per-upload server-side time from ~5.4s to ~3.5s at p50
104	2. Kubo pin worker configuration is tuned for concurrent load — pin latency under 50 concurrent clients does not exceed 2x the single-client baseline
105	3. Load test results (same mixed workload scenario, same staging hardware) show measurable throughput improvement over Phase 19 baselines (>15% ops/s increase at 50+ clients)
106	4. Latency improvements are validated with before/after Prometheus histogram comparisons and documented in baselines
107	
108	**Plans:** 4/4 plans complete
109	
110	Plans:
111	
112	- [x] 19.2-01-PLAN.md -- Capture pre-optimization baselines, parallelize SDK upload pin orchestration with Promise.allSettled
113	- [x] 19.2-02-PLAN.md -- Switch Kubo datastore to pebbleds, capture post-optimization baselines with before/after comparison
114	- [x] 19.2-03-PLAN.md -- Register PERF-09 in REQUIREMENTS.md (gap closure)
115	- [x] 19.2-04-PLAN.md -- Three-point local performance baselines with matched-environment comparison (gap closure)
116	
117	### Phase 20: Vault Migration
118	
119	**Goal**: The server stores zero crypto material -- rootFolderKey lives in the IPFS vault blob, making the server a true zero-knowledge relay
120	**Depends on**: Phase 19 (IPNS must be reliable before making it a login-adjacent dependency)

- **Milestones** — `Phases 18-22 (in progress)` → **`Phases 18-49 (completed 2026-06-18)`**.

- **Overview** — rewritten to past tense, noting the milestone completed and that scope expanded well beyond the original four thrusts (SDK extraction, writable shares, FUSE durability, TEE migration, release engineering).
